<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for yogic practices, specifically focusing on the Fourth Chapter regarding the Yoni-Mudrâ, a technique used to redirect internal energy.</meta>

## CHAPTER IV.
### Yoni-Mudrâ. The Sacred Drink of the Kaulas.

->First, by the practice of inhaling, one should fill the mind within the base-center. The "Yoni" is located between the anus and the genitals; by contracting it, the practice begins.<- <note>original: "आदौ पूरक योगेन..."</note>

FIRST with a strong inspiration fix the mind in the <term>ádhár lotus</term> <note>The Muladhara or root chakra at the base of the spine.</note>. Then engage in contracting the **Yoni**, which is situated in the perineal space.

->Meditating on the God of Love dwelling in the sacred source of Brahma, resembling the Bandhuka flower, brilliant as ten million suns and cool as ten million moons. Above that is a subtle flame, the form of consciousness, the supreme art. One should imagine themselves becoming one with that flame.<- <note>original: "ब्रह्मयोनिगतं ध्यात्वा..."</note>

2 There let him contemplate that the God of Love resides in that <term>Brahma Yoni</term> <note>The "Source of Brahma," a symbolic point of creation within the root center.</note> and that he is beautiful like the **Bandhuk flower** <note>The *Pentapetes Phoenicia*, a scarlet-colored flower that blooms at noon.</note>—brilliant as tens of millions of suns, and cool as tens of millions of moons. Above this (Yoni) is a very small and subtle flame, whose form is intelligence. Then let him imagine that a union takes place there between himself and that flame (the <term>Śiva</term> and <term>Śakti</term> <note>The masculine and feminine divine principles whose union represents the highest state of consciousness.</note>).

->Truly, it proceeds through the path of Brahma, through the sequence of the three signs. That nectar is situated in heaven, characterized by supreme bliss. It is white-red, rich in splendor, showering streams of nectar. Having drunk the divine nectar of the Kula, one should enter the Kula again.<- <note>original: "गच्छति ब्रह्ममार्गेण..."</note>

3. (Then imagine that)—There go up through the <term>Sushumnâ</term> <note>The central channel of the subtle body through which spiritual energy rises.</note> vessel, the three bodies in their due order (*i.e.*, the etheric, the astral and the mental bodies). There is emitted in every <term>chakra</term> <note>An energy center or "wheel" located along the spine.</note> the nectar, the characteristic of which is great bliss. Its colour is whitish rosy (pink), full of splendour, showering down in jets the immortal fluid. Let him drink this wine of immortality which is divine, and then again enter the <term>Kulâ</term> <note>The "Kula" refers here to the base-center or the source of the spiritual lineage.</note> (*i.e.*, perineal space.)

Note.—While these subtle bodies go up, they drink at every stage this nectar, called <term>Kulâmrita</term> <note>The "Nectar of the Kula," a symbolic fluid representing spiritual ecstasy.</note>.

->One should return to the Kula again through the practice of rhythmic breath, and not otherwise. And that is called "Life" by me in this scripture.<- <note>original: "पुनरेव कुलं गच्छेन्..."</note>

4. Then let him go again to the **Kulâ** through the practice of <term>mátrá Yoga</term> <note>A form of yoga focused on the rhythmic timing of the breath.</note> (*i.e.*, <term>prâṇâyâma</term> <note>The yogic practice of breath control.</note>.) This Yoni has been called by me in the <term>Tantras</term> <note>A category of esoteric Indian scriptures dealing with ritual and meditation.</note> as equal to life.

<summary>This page describes the Yoni-Mudrâ practice, detailing a visualization where the practitioner moves energy and consciousness from the root center up through the central channel to experience spiritual nectar.</summary>
<keywords>Yoni-Mudrâ, Kaula tradition, root chakra, nectar of immortality, Sushumna, breath control, Shiva and Shakti</keywords>

--- Page 48 ---
<page-num>42</page-num>

# SIVA SAMHITA

->Again, let him be absorbed in that Womb, which is the essence of the Fire of Time and the Auspicious One.<-
->This is the supreme <term>Yoni-mudra</term> <note>literally "Womb-Seal," a physical and energetic practice intended to internalize the life force</note>; its lock has been proclaimed.<-
->By the mere mastery of its lock, there is nothing that cannot be achieved. 5.<-

5. Again, let the practitioner be absorbed in that <term>Yoni</term>, where dwells the fire of death—the nature of <term>Shiva</term> <note>the Destroyer and transformer in the Hindu trinity</note>, and so forth. Thus has been described by me the method of practicing the great <term>Yoni-Mudrâ</term>. From success in its practice, there is nothing which cannot be accomplished.

->Even those <term>mantras</term> <note>sacred chants or sound-syllables</note> which are severed, locked, or paralyzed;<-
->Burned <term>mantras</term>, those without a flame, or those that are soiled and rejected;<-
->The dull, the infantile, the aged, the proud, or those in the prime of youth;<-
->Those aligned with the enemy's side, those without vigor, and those lacking essence;<-
->Those deprived of strength, or broken into a hundred pieces;<-
->When joined with this method, they quickly become effective.<-
->All these grant power and liberation when directed by the <term>Guru</term> <note>spiritual teacher</note>.<-
->After initiating the student according to the rules and purifying him a thousand times,<-
->This <term>mudra</term> is proclaimed so that the student may be fit to hold the power of the <term>mantra</term>. 6.<-

6. Even those <term>mantras</term> which are deformed (<term>chhinna</term> <note>broken</note>) or paralyzed (<term>kilita</term> <note>locked</note>), scorched (<term>stambhita</term> <note>halted</note>) by fire, or whose flame has become attenuated, or which are dark and ought to be abandoned, or which are evil, or too old, or which are proud of their budding youth, or have gone over to the side of the enemy, or weak and essenceless without vitality; or which have been divided into hundreds of parts—even they become fertile through time and method. All these can give powers and emancipation when properly given to the disciple by the <term>Guru</term>, after having initiated him according to proper rites and bathed him a thousand times. This <term>Yoni-mudrâ</term> has been described in order that the student may deserve to be initiated into the mysteries of and receive the <term>mantras</term>.

->Though he were to kill thousands of <term>Brahmins</term> <note>priests or scholars; considered the most heinous sin in traditional law</note> or destroy the three worlds,<-
->He is not stained by sin when bound by the <term>Yoni-mudra</term>. 7.<-

7. He who practices <term>Yoni-Mudrâ</term> is not polluted by sin, were he to murder a thousand <term>Brahmins</term> or kill all the inhabitants of the three worlds <note>Heaven, Earth, and the Underworld</note>.

->Though he kills his teacher, drinks wine, steals, or violates the teacher's bed,<-
->He is not bound by these sins through the practice of <term>Yoni-mudra</term>. 8.<-

8. Were he to kill his teacher or drink wine or commit theft, or violate the bed of his preceptor <note>these represent the "Great Sins" or <term>Mahapatakas</term> in ancient Indian ethics</note>, he is not stained by these sins also, by virtue of this <term>mudrâ</term> <note>this hyperbolic language is often used in tantric texts to emphasize that the spiritual transformation of the practice is so profound that it transcends all worldly karma</note>.

<summary>This page concludes the description of Yoni-mudra, asserting its power to revitalize "defective" mantras and its ability to spiritually purify a practitioner from even the most severe moral transgressions.</summary>
<keywords>Yoni-mudra, Mantra, Guru, Shiva, Purification, Sin, Siddhi, Tantra</keywords>

# CHAPTER IV. 9-14.

->Therefore, those who desire liberation should constantly perform practice.<-
->From practice, success is born; through practice, one attains liberation. || 9 ||<-

9. Therefore, those who wish for emancipation should practise this daily. Through practice (<note>original: *abhyâsa*</note>), success is obtained; through practice one gains liberation.

->One gains consciousness through practice; Yoga proceeds from practice.<-
->Success in the seals comes from practice; through practice comes the mastery of breath.<-
->The cheating of time comes from practice, and thus one becomes the conqueror of death. || 10 ||<-

10. Perfect consciousness is gained through practice. Yoga is attained through practice; success in <term>Mudrâs</term> <note>symbolic gestures or "seals" used to direct energy</note> comes by practice; through practice is gained success in <term>prânâyáma</term> <note>the yogic art of breath control</note>. Death can be cheated of its prey through practice, and man becomes the conqueror of death by practice.

->The power of prophecy and the ability to go anywhere at will arise from the yoga of practice.<-
->The seal of the womb is to be kept strictly secret; it should not be given to just anyone.<-
->In no way should it be revealed, even if life itself is at risk. || 11 ||<-

11. Through practice one gets the power of <term>vách</term> <note>divine speech or the power of prophecy</note>, and the power of going everywhere, through mere exertion of will. This <term>Yoni-mudrâ</term> <note>literally the "womb seal," a practice used to internalize consciousness</note> should be kept in great secrecy, and not be given to everybody. Even when threatened with death, it should not be revealed or given to others.

### *The Awakening of Kundalini.*

->Now I shall describe the highest means for achieving success in Yoga.<-
->It is to be kept secret by the accomplished ones; this Yoga is very difficult to obtain. || 12 ||<-

12. Now I shall tell you the best means of attaining success in Yoga. The practitioners should keep it secret. It is the most inaccessible Yoga.

->When the sleeping Goddess, the Coiled Power, is awakened by the grace of the Teacher,<-
->Then all the lotuses and the knots are pierced through. || 13 ||<-

13. When the sleeping goddess <term>Kundalini</term> <note>the latent spiritual energy envisioned as a coiled serpent at the base of the spine</note> is awakened, through the grace of the <term>Guru</term> <note>spiritual guide or teacher</note>, then all the lotuses <note>the *chakras* or energy centers of the body</note> and the bonds <note>the *granthis* or psychic knots that hinder spiritual progress</note> are readily pierced through and through.

->Therefore, with every effort, to awaken the Goddess<-
->Sleeping at the mouth of the Opening of Brahman, one should perform the practice of the seals. || 14 ||<-

14. Therefore, in order that the goddess, who is asleep in the mouth of the <term>Brahmarandhra</term> <note>the "Opening of Brahman" at the crown of the head</note> (the innermost hollow of the <term>Sushumnâ</term> <note>the central channel of the nervous system/subtle body</note>) be awakened, the <term>Mudrâs</term> should be practised with the greatest care.

<summary>This page emphasizes that spiritual liberation and supernatural powers are achieved only through persistent practice (abhyasa). It introduces the awakening of the Kundalini energy as the primary goal of these techniques, which allows the practitioner to pierce through internal spiritual blockages.</summary>
<keywords>Abhyasa, Mudra, Pranayama, Kundalini, Guru, Brahmarandhra, spiritual liberation, yoga practice, Yoni-mudra</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page which detailed the preparations for advanced Yoga, this page introduces the specific physical seals or gestures known as Mudras.</meta>

<page-num>44</page-num>
# SIVA SAMHITA.

->The Great Seal (Mahamudra), the Great Bond (Mahabandha), the Great Piercing (Mahavedha), and the Sky-walking (Khechari);<-
<note>original: "महामुद्रा महाबन्धो महावेधश्च खेचरी"</note>
->The Throat-clearing (Jalandhara), the Root Bond (Mulabandha), and similarly the Inversion (Viparitkarana);<-
<note>original: "जालंधरो मूलबंधो विपरीतकृतिस्तथा"</note>
->The Upward Fly (Uddana), the Thunderbolt (Vajroni), and tenth, the Arousal of Power (Shaktichalana).<-
<note>original: "उड्डानं चैव वज्रोणी दशमे शक्तिचालनम्"</note>
->These indeed are the ten <term>Mudras</term>, the highest and best among all seals.<-
<note>original: "इदं हि मुद्रादशकं मुद्राणामुत्तमोत्तमम्"</note>

15. Out of the many <term>Mudras</term> <note>A "seal" or ritual gesture used in Yoga to lock energy within the body</note>, the following ten are the best:— (1) Mahamudra, (2) Mahabandha, (3) Mahavedha, (4) Khechari, (5) Jalandhara, (6) Mulabandha, (7) Viparitkarana, (8) Uddana, (9) Vajroni, and (10) Shaktichalana.

### Now, the Description of the Great Seal (Mahamudra).

->My dearest, I shall now describe to you the Great Seal in this <term>Tantra</term>,<-
<note>original: "महामुद्रां प्रवक्ष्यामि तन्त्रेऽस्मिन्मम वल्लभे"</note>
->By attaining which the perfected ones like the sage Kapila and others obtained success in the past.<-
<note>original: "यां प्राप्य सिद्धाः सिद्धिं च कपिलाद्याः पुरागताः"</note>

16. My dearest, I shall now describe to you the <term>Mahamudra</term> <note>The "Great Seal," considered one of the most important practices in Hatha Yoga</note>, from whose knowledge the ancient sages <term>Kapila</term> <note>The legendary founder of the Samkhya school of philosophy</note> and others obtained success in <term>Yoga</term>.

#### (1.)—Mahâ-Mudrâ.

->Pressing the perineum carefully with the left heel, according to the teacher's instruction,<-
<note>original: "अपसव्येन संपीड्य पादमूलेन सादरम् । गुरूपदेशतो योनिं"</note>
->Stretching out the right leg and holding it fast with both hands;<-
<note>original: "सव्यं प्रसारितं पादं धृत्वा पाणियुगेन वै"</note>
->Having closed the nine gates of the body and placing the chin on the chest;<-
<note>original: "नवद्वाराणि संयम्य चिबुकं हृदयेपरि"</note>
->Focusing the mind on the path of consciousness, the breath practice becomes effective.<-
<note>original: "चित्तं चित्तपथे दत्त्वा प्रभवेद्वायुसाधनम्"</note>
->This is the Great Seal, kept secret in all the sacred texts.<-
<note>original: "महामुद्राभवेदेषा सर्वतन्त्रेषु गोपिता"</note>
->Having practiced it on the left side, one should then practice it on the right;<-
<note>original: "वामाङ्गेन समभ्यस्य दक्षाङ्गेनाभ्यसेत्पुनः"</note>
->The practitioner, with a controlled mind, should perform the breath regulation equally.<-
<note>original: "प्राणायामं समं कृत्वा योगी नियतमानसः"</note>

17. In accordance with the instructions of the <term>Guru</term> <note>A spiritual teacher</note>, press gently the <term>perineum</term> <note>The area between the anus and the genitals</note> with the heel of the left foot. Stretching the right foot out, hold it fast by the two hands. Having closed the <term>nine gates</term> <note>The nine openings of the human body: two eyes, two ears, two nostrils, the mouth, the anus, and the genitals</note>, place the chin on the chest. Then concentrate the vibrations of the mind and inspire air and retain it by <term>kumbhaka</term> <note>Breath retention</note> (so long as one can comfortably keep it). This is the <term>Mahamudra</term>, held secret in all the <term>Tantras</term> <note>Esoteric scriptures detailing yogic and ritual practices</note>. The steady-minded <term>Yogi</term>, having practised it on the left side, should then practise it on the right side; and in all cases must be firm in <term>pranayama</term>—the regulation of his breath.

->By this method, even an unfortunate practitioner achieves success;<-
<note>original: "अनेन विधिना योगी मन्दभाग्योपि सिध्यति"</note>
->It causes the movement of all the energy channels and the preservation of the vital essence.<-
<note>original: "सर्वासामेव नाडीनां चालनं बिन्दुमरणम्"</note>
->It is the very life of the student, a destroyer of sins;<-
<note>original: "जीवनन्तु कषायस्य पातकानां विनाशनम्"</note>
->It cures all diseases and increases the internal digestive fire.<-
<note>original: "सर्वरोगोपशमनं जठराग्निविवर्धनम्"</note>

<summary>This page lists the ten primary Mudras (yogic seals) and provides a detailed instructional guide on the first and most important, the Mahamudra, including its physical execution and spiritual benefits.</summary>
<keywords>Mahamudra, Yoga, Mudras, Guru, Pranayama, Kapila, Tantra, breath retention, physical health, spiritual success</keywords>

# CHAPTER IV. 18-21.

---

->With a body of stainless beauty, destroying old age and death,<-
->Bringing the fruit of desired aims, happiness, and the subduing of the senses.<-
->All these things mentioned are attained by the Yogi established in yoga;<-
->They shall surely come through practice—of this there is no doubt. ॥ 18 ॥<-

18. In this way, even the most unfortunate <term>Yogi</term> <note>A practitioner of the disciplines of Yoga</note> might obtain success. By this means all the vessels of the body are roused and stirred into activity; the life-force is increased and its decay is checked, and all sins are destroyed. All diseases are healed, and the gastric fire <note>original: 'jatharagni'; the internal heat responsible for digestion and metabolism</note> is increased. It gives faultless beauty to the body, and destroys decay and death. All fruits of desires and pleasures are obtained, and the senses are conquered. The <term>Yogi</term> fixed in meditation acquires all the above-mentioned things through practice. There should be no hesitation in doing so.

->This Mudra should be concealed with great effort, O Worshipped of the Gods!<-
->By attaining it, Yogis cross to the further shore of the ocean of worldly existence. ॥ 19 ॥<-

19. O ye worshipped of the gods! Know that this <term>Mudrâ</term> <note>A symbolic or ritual gesture or internal seal used in yoga to direct energy</note> is to be kept secret with the greatest care. Obtaining this, the <term>Yogi</term> crosses the ocean of the world <note>original: 'bhavambhodheh'; a metaphor for the cycle of suffering and rebirth</note>.

->This Mudra is indeed a wish-fulfilling cow for practitioners, as declared by me;<-
->It should be performed with secret practice and not given to just anyone. ॥ 20 ॥<-

20. This <term>Mudrâ</term>, described by me, is the giver of all desires to the practitioner; it should be practised in secrecy, and ought never to be given to everybody.

### (2).---*The Great Binding (Mahâ-Bandha).*

### Now begins the description of the Great Binding.

->Then, having extended the feet, place one upon the thigh;<-
->Contract the perineum, making the downward-breath move upward;<-
->Joining it with the balancing-breath, and making the life-breath move downward;<-
->The wise one should bind them for the sake of upward movement, with life-breath and downward-breath;<-
->This is called the Great Binding, which bestows the path to perfection.<-
->The collection of essences from the network of vessels goes to the head of the Yogi;<-
->One should practice this with both feet, one by one, with great effort. ॥ 21 ॥<-

21. Then (after <term>Mâhamudrâ</term> or "The Great Seal"), having extended the (right) foot, place it on the (left) thigh; contract the <term>perineum</term> <note>the region between the genitals and the anus, referred to here as 'guda-yoni'</note>, and draw the <term>apâna vâyû</term> <note>the downward-moving vital energy</note> upwards and join it with the <term>samâna vâyu</term> <note>the balancing energy located in the abdominal region</note>; bend the <term>prâna vâyû</term> <note>the inward and upward-moving vital breath</note> downwards, and then let the wise <term>Yogi</term> bind them in trinity in the navel (that is, the <term>prâna</term> and the <term>apâna</term> should be joined with the <term>Samâna</term> in the navel). This practice causes the vital fluids to rise through the network of channels to the head. One should practice this carefully using each leg in turn.

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on the benefits of the first Mudra, emphasizing its secrecy and health benefits, before introducing the technical instructions for Mahā-Bandha (The Great Binding).</summary>
<keywords>Maha-Bandha, Mudra, Yogi, Prana, Apana, Samana, vital energy, gastric fire, immortality, yoga anatomy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions on Hatha Yoga seals and locks, specifically concluding the Mahâbandha and introducing the Mahâ-Vedha. The numbering follows the previous sequence of techniques.</meta>

-># SHIVA SAMHITA<-

I have told you now the <term>Mahâbandha</term> <note>The "Great Bind," a posture where the practitioner locks specific energy points to direct life force</note>, which shows the way to liberation. By this, all the fluids in the vessels of the body of the Yogi are propelled towards the head. This should be practised with great care, alternately with both feet.

> Through practice, the breath enters the middle channel of the <term>Sushumnâ</term> <note>The central spiritual channel of the body, located along the spine</note>. By this, the body is nourished, the skeleton is firmly strengthened, and the heart of the Yogi becomes full of cheerfulness. Through this Great Bind, the master of Yoga achieves all his desires. || 22 ||
<note>original Sanskrit: "bhavedabhyāsato vāyuḥ suṣumṇāṃ madhyasaṅgataḥ... bandhanānena yogīndraḥ sādhayētsarvamīpsitam"</note>

### (3.) -- Mahâ-Vedha

#### Now begins the description of Mahâ-Vedha (The Great Piercing).

> "O goddess of the three worlds! <note>Shiva is traditionally addressing his consort, Parvati</note> When the Yogi, while performing the Great Bind, causes the union of the <term>prâna</term> <note>the upward-moving life energy</note> and <term>apâna</term> <note>the downward-moving life energy</note> breaths, and filling the abdomen with air, drives it slowly towards the buttocks, it is called <term>Mahâ-Vedha</term>." || 23 ||
<note>original Sanskrit: "apānaprāṇayoraikyaṃ kṛtvā tribhuvaneśvari... sphicau saṃtāḍayeddhimānvedho'yaṃ kīrtito mayā"</note>

24. The best of the Yogis having, through the help of the <term>vâyu</term> <note>breath or life-wind</note>, pierced with this "perforator" the knot which is in the path of the <term>Sushumnâ</term>, should then pierce the <term>Brahma-granthi</term> <note>The "Knot of Brahma," the first of three major energetic blockages that prevent the ascent of spiritual energy</note>.

25. He who always practices this <term>Mahâ-Vedha</term> in great secrecy obtains <term>vâyu-siddhi</term> <note>success or mastery over the breath</note>. It destroys decay and death.

26. The gods residing in the <term>chakras</term> <note>energy centers in the subtle body</note> tremble owing to the gentle influx and efflux of air in <term>prânâyâma</term> <note>controlled breathing exercises</note>; the great goddess <term>Kundalini</term> <note>the primordial energy coiled at the base of the spine; here called Kunali Mahâ Mâyâ</note> is also absorbed into the mount <term>Kailâsa</term> <note>the crown chakra at the top of the head, named after the sacred mountain</note>.

27. The <term>Mahâmudrâ</term> <note>The Great Seal</note> and the <term>Mahâbandha</term> <note>The Great Bind</note> are fruitless without the <term>Mahâ-Vedha</term> <note>The Great Piercing</note>. Therefore, the Yogi should practice all three in order with great effort.

<summary>This page describes the transition from Mahâbandha to Mahâ-Vedha, a technique used to "pierce" energetic knots in the body and force life energy into the central channel. It emphasizes that these three major practices (mudra, bandha, and vedha) must be performed together to be effective.</summary>

<keywords>Mahabandha, Mahavedha, Sushumna, Kundalini, Brahma-granthi, Pranayama, Vayu-siddhi, Chakras, energy locks</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for advanced yogic practices, specifically transitioning from the combination of the three great seals (Mahamudra, Mahabandha, and Mahavedha) to the description of the Khechari Mudra.</meta>

### CHAPTER IV. 27-32. 47

27. The <term>Great Seal</term> <note>original: Mahâmudra</note> and the <term>Great Lock</term> <note>original: Mahâbandha</note> become fruitless if they are not followed by the <term>Great Piercing</term> <note>original: Mahâ-vedha</note>; therefore, the <term>Yogi</term> should practice all these three successively with great care.

->**Whoever performs these three with effort four times a day,**<-
->**undoubtedly conquers death within six months.**<-
<note>original: एतत्त्रयं प्रयत्नेन चतुर्वारं करोति यः । षण्मासाभ्यन्तरं मृत्युं जयत्येव न संशयः ॥ २८ ॥</note>

28. He who practices these three daily four times with great care, undoubtedly conquers death within six months.

->**Only the perfected master knows the greatness of these three, and no one else;**<-
->**knowing these, all practitioners truly attain perfection.**<-
<note>original: एतत्त्रयस्य माहात्म्यं सिद्धो जानाति नेतरः । यज्ज्ञात्वा साधकाः सर्वे सिद्धिं सम्यग्लभन्ति वै ॥ २९ ॥</note>

29. Only the <term>perfected master</term> <note>Sanskrit: siddha; one who has attained supernatural powers</note> knows the importance of these three and no one else; knowing these, the practitioner obtains all success.

->**This must be kept secret with great effort by those desiring success;**<-
->**otherwise, perfection will not be achieved; this is the certainty regarding the seals.**<-
<note>original: गोपनीया प्रयत्नेन साधकैः सिद्धिमीप्सुभिः । अन्यथा च न सिद्धिः स्यान्मुद्राणामेष निश्चयः ॥ ३० ॥</note>

30. This should be kept in great secrecy by the practitioner desirous of obtaining power; otherwise, it is certain that the coveted powers can never be obtained through the practice of <term>seals</term> <note>Sanskrit: Mudrâs; symbolic gestures or energetic seals used in yoga</note>.

->(4.)—The Sky-Walking Seal <note>original: Khechari</note><-

->**Now follows the description of the Sky-Walking Seal.**<-
<note>original: अथ खेचरीमुद्राकथनम्</note>

->**The wise one, sitting in the Diamond Posture and free from all disturbances,**<-
->**should firmly fix the gaze between the two eyebrows.**<-
->**Turning the tongue backward into the hollow above the throat,**<-
->**the expert should carefully place it in the well of nectar.**<-
->**This is called the Sky-Walking Seal, described here for the benefit of devotees.**<-
<note>original: भ्रुवोरन्तर्गतां दृष्टिं विधाय सुदृढां सुधीः । उपविश्यासने वज्रे नानोपद्रववर्जितः ॥ लम्बिकोर्ध्वं स्थिते गर्ते रसनां विपरीतगाम् । संयोजयेत्प्रयत्नेन सुधाकूपे विचक्षणः । मुद्रैषा खेचरी प्रोक्ता भक्तानामनुरोधतः ॥ ३१ ॥</note>

31. The wise Yogi, sitting in the <term>Diamond Posture</term> <note>original: vajrâsana</note>, in a place free from all disturbance, should firmly fix his gaze on the spot in the middle of the two eyebrows; and reversing the tongue backwards, fix it in the hollow under the epi-glottis, placing it with great care on the mouth of the well of nectar (that is, closing up the air passage). This seal, described by me at the request of my devotees, is the <term>Sky-Walking Seal</term> <note>original: Khechari-Mudrâ</note>.

->**This is the mother of all perfections, dearer to me than life;**<-
->**through constant practice, one should drink the nectar daily.**<-
->**By this, one attains mastery over the physical body,**<-
->**becoming like a lion to the elephant of death.**<-
<note>original: सिद्धीनां जननी ह्येषा मम प्राणाधिकप्रिया । निरन्तरकृताभ्यासात्पीयूषं प्रत्यहं पिबेत् ॥ तेन विग्रहसिद्धिः स्यान्मृत्युमातङ्गकेसरी ॥ ३२ ॥</note>

32. O, my beloved! Know this to be the source of all success; always practicing it, let him drink the ambrosia daily. By this he obtains <term>mastery over the physical body</term> <note>original: vigraha-siddhi</note>, even as a lion triumphs over the elephant of death.

<summary>This page concludes the instructions on the three foundational yogic seals and introduces the Khechari Mudra (Sky-Walking Seal), a practice involving internal tongue placement and focused gaze to achieve physical mastery and immortality.</summary>
<keywords>Great Seal, Great Lock, Great Piercing, Sky-Walking Seal, Khechari Mudra, Diamond Posture, immortality, yoga nectar, mastery over the body</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the primary Mudras (seals) in Hatha Yoga. It concludes the section on Khechari Mudra, focusing on its spiritual benefits and the requirement for secrecy, and introduces Jalandhara Bandha, the throat lock used to preserve the body's internal 'nectar'.</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA.

<page-num>48</page-num>

->**Whether one is impure or pure, or in whatever state one may be, if their Khecharī is perfected, they are undoubtedly pure.**<- <note>original: "apavitraḥ pavitro vā sarvāvasthāṃ gato’pivā | khecarī yasya śuddhā tu sa śuddho nātra saṃśayaḥ ||"</note>

33. Whether pure or impure, in whatever condition one may be, if success be obtained in <term>Khecharî</term> <note>A practice where the tongue is turned back into the nasal cavity to stimulate higher consciousness</note>, he becomes pure. There is no doubt of it.

->**He who practices this for even half a moment, having crossed the great ocean of sin and enjoyed divine pleasures, is reborn into a virtuous family.**<- <note>original: "kṣaṇārdhaṃ kurute yastu tīrtvā pāpamahārṇavam | divyabhogānbhuktvā ca satkule sa prajāyate ||"</note>

34. He who practises it even for a moment crosses the great ocean of sins, and having enjoyed the pleasures of the <term>Deva-world</term> <note>The celestial realm or the dimension of the gods</note> is born into a noble family.

->**The wise man who practices this Khecharī Mudrā with a steady mind and without laziness perceives the passing of a hundred Brahmās as only half a moment.**<- <note>original: "mudraiṣā khecarī yastu svasthacitto hyatandritaḥ | śatabrahmagatenāpi kṣaṇārdhaṃ manyate hi saḥ ||"</note>

35. He who practises this <term>Khecharî-Mudrâ</term> <note>The 'Sky-Moving' seal</note> calmly and without laziness counts as seconds the period of a hundred Brahmâs <note>Brahmā is the creator deity; a 'hundred Brahmâs' refers to a vast, almost eternal cosmic cycle</note>.

->**The wise man who knows this Khecharī Mudrā through the instructions of a Guru reaches the highest state, even if he has been intent on various sins.**<- <note>original: "gurūpadeśato mudrāṃ yo vetti khecarīmimām | nānāpāparato dhīmān sa yāti paramāṃ gatim ||"</note>

36. He knows this <term>Khecharî-Mudrâ</term> according to the instructions of his <term>Guru</term> <note>A spiritual teacher or guide</note>, obtains the highest end, though immersed in great sins.

->**This Mudrā is as dear as life itself; it should not be given to just anyone. It should be concealed with great effort, O Adored of the Gods!**<- <note>original: "sā prāṇasadṛśī mudrā yasminkasminna dīyate | pracchādyate prayatnena mudreyaṃ surapūjite ||"</note>

37. O, ye adored of gods! this <term>Mudrâ</term>, dear as life, should not be given to everybody; it should be kept concealed with great care.

->(5.)—*Jâlandhara*.<-

->**Now, the Jâlandhara Bandha (The Throat Lock).**<- <note>original: "atha jālandharabandha"</note>

->**Having contracted the network of vessels in the throat, one should place the chin on the breast. This lock is called Jâlandhara, and it is rare even for the gods to achieve. The fire at the navel of living beings consumes the flood of nectar falling from the thousand-petalled lotus; for that reason, one should apply this lock.**<- <note>original: "badhvāgalaśirājālaṃ hṛdaye cibukaṃ nyaset | bandhojālandharaḥ prokto devānāmapi durlabhaḥ || nābhisthavahnirjantūnāṃ sahasrakamalacyutam | pibetpīyūṣavistāraṃ tadarthaṃ bandhayedimam ||"</note>

38. Having contracted the muscles of the throat press the chin on the breast. This is said to be the <term>Jâlandhara-Mudrâ</term>. Even gods reckon it as inestimable. The fire in the region of the navel (i.e., the <term>gastric juice</term> <note>In yogic anatomy, this digestive fire or 'Agni' is said to consume the vital essence if not regulated</note>) drinks the nectar which exudes out of the <term>thousand-petalled lotus</term> <note>The Sahasrara Chakra at the crown of the head, viewed as a source of divine nectar or Amrita</note>. [In order to prevent the nectar to be thus consumed], he should practise this <term>Bandha</term> <note>A physical 'lock' used to redirect or seal energy within the body</note>.

->**By this lock, the wise man drinks the nectar himself; having attained immortality, he rejoices in the three worlds.**<- <note>original: "bandhenānena pīyūṣaṃ svayaṃ pibati buddhimān | amaratvañca samprāpya modate bhuvanatraye ||"</note>

39. By this lock, the wise man drinks the nectar himself; having attained immortality, he rejoices in the three worlds.

<summary>This page describes the profound spiritual benefits of Khechari Mudra, including purification and the transcendence of time, and introduces Jalandhara Bandha, a technique to prevent the body's internal nectar from being consumed by the digestive fire.</summary>
<keywords>Khecharî, Mudrâ, Jâlandhara-Mudrâ, Bandha, Guru, nectar, Amrita, Sahasrara, digestive fire, purification, immortality</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of yogic locks (Bandhas) and seals (Mudras) from Chapter IV, transitioning from the Jalandhara Bandha to the Mula Bandha and the Viparit-karana.</meta>

### CHAPTER IV. 39-45. 49

39. Through this **Bandha** <note>a "lock" or internal energetic contraction</note>, the wise **Yogi** himself drinks the nectar, and, obtaining immortality, enjoys the three worlds <note>the physical, astral, and causal realms</note>.

-> **This Jalandhara Bandha is the giver of success to the perfected ones;** <note>original Sanskrit: "Jālandharo bandha eṣa siddhānāṃ siddhidāyakaḥ"</note> <-
-> **it is practiced daily by the practitioner who desires success. 40.** <note>original Sanskrit: "Abhyāsaḥ kriyate nityaṃ yoginā siddhimicchatā"</note> <-

40. This <term>Jâlandhara-Bandha</term> <note>the Throat Lock, where the chin is pressed against the chest</note> is the giver of success to the practitioner; the **Yogi** desirous of success should practise it daily.

-> (6.)—*Mula-Bandha.* <-
-> **Now, the Root Lock.** <note>original Sanskrit: "Atha mūlabandhaḥ"</note> <-

-> **Pressing the perineum firmly with the heel,** <note>original Sanskrit: "Pādamūlena saṃpīḍya gudamārgaṃ suyantritam"</note> <-
-> **one should forcibly draw the downward-moving air upward through practice.** <-
-> **This is known as the Root Lock, the destroyer of decay and death. 41.** <-

41. Pressing well the anus with the heel, forcibly draw upwards the <term>apâna vâyu</term> <note>the vital energy that governs the lower abdomen and elimination</note> slowly by practice. This is described as the <term>Mula-Bandha</term>—the destroyer of decay and death.

-> **It achieves the union of the downward and upward vital breaths;** <note>original Sanskrit: "Apānaprāṇayoraikyaṃ प्रकरोत्यधिकल्पितम्"</note> <-
-> **Through this lock, the Seal of the Source is surely perfected. 42.** <-

42. If, in the course of the practice of this <term>Mudrâ</term> <note>a "seal" or symbolic gesture used to direct energy</note>, the **Yogi** can unite the <term>apâna</term> with the <term>prâna vâyu</term> <note>the vital breath of the upper chest and heart region</note>, then it becomes of course the <term>Yoni-Mudrâ</term>.

-> **Once the Seal of the Source is perfected, what cannot be achieved on this earth?** <note>original Sanskrit: "Siddhāyāṃ yonimudrāyāṃ kiṃ na sidhyati bhūtale"</note> <-
-> **By the grace of this lock, having conquered all lethargy in the sky,** <-
-> **The practitioner, seated in the Lotus Posture, leaves the ground and remains suspended. 43.** <-

43. He who has accomplished <term>Yoni-Mudrâ</term> <note>a practice focused on the pelvic floor and internal energy cultivation</note>, what can he not accomplish in this world? Sitting in the <term>padmâsana</term> <note>the Lotus Posture</note> posture, free from idleness, the **Yogi**, leaving the ground, moves through the air, by virtue of this <term>Mudrâ</term>.

-> **One should practice this lock in a well-hidden and solitary place,** <note>original Sanskrit: "Sugupte nirjane deśe bandhamenaṃ samabhyaset"</note> <-
-> **If the great practitioner desires to cross the ocean of worldly existence. 44.** <-

44. If the wise **Yogi** is desirous of crossing the ocean of the world <note>the cycle of birth and death known as Samsara</note>, let him practise this **Bandha** in secret, in a retired place.

-> (7.) *Viparit-karana.* <-
-> **Now, the Inverted Action Seal.** <note>original Sanskrit: "Atha viparītakaraṇī mudrā"</note> <-

-> **Placing the head on the ground, one should raise the two feet into the sky;** <note>original Sanskrit: "Bhūtale svaśirodattvā khe nayeccaraṇadvayam"</note> <-
-> **This Inverted Action is kept secret in all the esoteric texts. 45.** <note>original Sanskrit: "Viparītakṛtiścaiṣā sarvatantreṣu gopitā"</note> <-

45. Putting the head on the ground, let him stretch out his legs upwards, moving them round and round. This is <term>Viparit-karana</term> <note>literally "Inverted Action," usually a precursor to the headstand or shoulderstand</note>, kept secret in all the <term>Tantras</term> <note>spiritual texts detailing rituals and yogic techniques</note>.

<vocab>Bandha (lock), Yogi (practitioner), Jâlandhara-Bandha (throat lock), Mula-Bandha (root lock), apâna vâyu (downward energy), prâna vâyu (vital breath), Yoni-Mudrâ (seal of the source), padmâsana (lotus posture), Viparit-karana (inversion practice), Tantras (esoteric scriptures)</vocab>

These practices are described as essential for attaining success in yoga, including physical health, longevity, and supernatural abilities like levitation.</summary>

50
SIVA SAMHITA

->**Whoever performs this practice daily for three hours,**<-
->**that Yogi conquers death and is not destroyed even during the universal dissolution. <note>verse 46</note>**<-

46. The Yogi who practises it daily for three hours, conquers death, and is not destroyed even in the <term>Pralaya</term> <note>original: Pralaya; the periodic dissolution of the entire universe in Hindu cosmology</note>.

->**He who drinks the nectar reaches equality with the perfected masters;**<-
->**he who performs this lock is worthy of being served by all the worlds. <note>verse 47</note>**<-

47. He who drinks nectar becomes equal to <term>Siddhas</term> <note>original: Siddhas; perfected beings who have attained spiritual and physical powers</note>; he who practises this <term>Bandha</term> <note>original: Bandha; an internal "lock" or contraction used to control vital energy</note> becomes an adept among all creatures.

### (8.)—*The Upward Lock* <note>original: Uddâna-bandha; more commonly known today as Uddiyana Bandha</note>

->**One should stretch the area above and below the navel toward the spine;**<-
->**this is the Upward Lock, which destroys the entire flood of sorrows.**<-
->**One should perform the backward contraction of the abdomen above the navel;**<-
->**this is called the Upward Lock, a lion to the elephant of death. <note>verse 48</note>**<-

48. When the intestines above and below the navel are brought toward the back <note>contracted toward the spine</note>, it is called <term>Uddâna-Bandha</term>—the destroyer of all sins and sorrows. The viscera of the abdominal cavity should be pulled above the navel. This is <term>Uddâna-Bandha</term>, the "lion" that defeats the "elephant" of death.

->**The Yogi who always performs this four times every day,**<-
->**purifies his navel, by which the vital breath is perfected. <note>verse 49</note>**<-

49. The Yogi, who always practises it four times a day, purifies his navel, through which the winds <note>the vital breaths or Pranas</note> are purified.

->**By practicing for six months, the Yogi certainly conquers death;**<-
->**his digestive fire is kindled, and an increase of bodily fluids occurs. <note>verse 50</note>**<-

50. By practising it for six months, the Yogi certainly conquers death; the gastric fire is kindled, and there takes place an increase of the <gloss>essential secretions</gloss> of the body.

->**Through this, the perfection of the physical form is attained;**<-
->**the destruction of all diseases for the Yogi is certain. <note>verse 51</note>**<-

51. Through this, consequently, the <term>vigrahasiddhi</term> <note>original: vigrahasiddhi; the attainment of a perfected, disease-free, or even supernatural physical body</note> is also obtained. All the diseases of the Yogi are certainly destroyed by it.

->**Having obtained it from the Guru with effort, the wise one should practice;**<-
->**in a secluded, well-situated place, this most rare lock. <note>verse 52</note>**<-

52. Having learnt the method from the <term>Guru</term> <note>original: Guru; a spiritual teacher</note>, the wise Yogi should practise it with great care. This most inaccessible <term>Mudrâ</term> <note>original: Mudrâ; a "seal" or yogic gesture used to direct energy</note> should be practised in a retired and undisturbed place.

<summary>This page describes the benefits of a specific yogic practice (the Upward Lock or Uddiyana Bandha), claiming it can purify the body, kindle digestive fire, and allow the practitioner to conquer death.</summary>

# CHAPTER IV. 53-58. 51

## (10.)—*Shakti-chalan* <note>The practice of "Arousing the Power."</note>

### Now begins the Shakti-Chalan Mudra.

->The wise practitioner should forcibly and firmly move the <term>Kundalini</term> <note>the latent spiritual energy often described as a coiled serpent</note> who sleeps in the <term>root lotus</term> <note>original: *ādhāra-kamala*, the Muladhara chakra at the base of the spine</note>. This is done by drawing up the <term>downward-moving breath</term> <note>original: *apāna vāyu*</note>. This is the <term>Shakti-Chalan Mudra</term>, the giver of all powers. <-

53. Let the wise Yogi forcibly and firmly draw up the goddess <term>Kundalini</term> sleeping in the <term>root</term> <note>original: *ādhār*</note> lotus, by means of the <term>downward-moving breath</term> <note>original: *apāna vāyu*</note>. This is <term>Shakti-Chalan Mudra</term>, the giver of all powers.

->Indeed, whoever practices this <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> every day achieves an increase in lifespan and the destruction of all diseases. <-

54. He who practices this <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> daily, gets increase of life and destruction of diseases.

->When the "serpent" <note>the Kundalini</note> leaves her sleep, she truly moves upward on her own. Therefore, the Yogi who desires perfection should practice this diligently. <-

55. Leaving sleep, the serpent (*i.e.* the <term>Kundalini</term>) herself goes up; therefore let the Yogi desirous of power <note>original: *siddhi*</note> practice this.

->For the one who always practices this supreme <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> according to the instructions of their <term>Guru</term> <note>spiritual teacher</note>, the perfection of the physical body is achieved, bestowing powers such as <term>minuteness</term> <note>original: *animā*, the ability to become as small as an atom</note>. How could such a person fear death? <-

56. He who practices always this best <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> according to the instructions of his guru, obtains the <term>perfection of the body</term> <note>original: *vigraha-siddhi*</note>, which gives the powers of <term>minuteness</term> <note>original: *animā*</note>, etc., and has no fear of death.

->Success is not far off for the one who practices <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> with effort for two <term>muhurtas</term> <note>The Sanskrit text says "muhūrta-dvaya," which equals about 96 minutes. The translator's "two seconds" is likely a significant error or shorthand in the source text.</note> according to the rules. The Yogi should perform <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> while in a proper posture. <-

57. He who practices the <term>Shakti-Chalan</term> properly for two seconds <note>As noted above, the original Sanskrit specifies a much longer duration of roughly an hour and a half.</note>, and with care, is very near to success. This <term>Mudra</term> should be practiced by the Yogi in the proper posture.

->There never has been, nor ever shall be, anything equal to these ten <term>Mudras</term>. By the practice of even one of them, a person becomes a <term>siddha</term> <note>a perfected master</note> and achieves success; it cannot be otherwise. <-

58. These are the ten <term>Mudras</term> whose equal there never was nor ever shall be: through the practice of any one of them, a person becomes a <term>siddha</term> and obtains success.

->**Thus ends the fourth chapter, titled "The Description of the Mudras," in the Shiva Samhita, the dialogue between Shiva and Gauri.** <note>original: *iti śrī-śiva-saṃhitāyāṃ hara-gaurī-saṃvāde mudrā-kathanaṃ nāma caturtha-paṭalaḥ samāptaḥ*</note><-

<note>[The <term>Vajroli Mudra</term> described in this chapter in the original is omitted here, as it is an obscene practice indulged in by low class Tantrists, Translator.]</note>

<summary>This page concludes the fourth chapter of the Shiva Samhita by describing the Shakti-Chalan Mudra, a practice intended to awaken the Kundalini energy at the base of the spine. It also marks the end of the section on the ten primary yogic seals (Mudras), claiming they are the ultimate tools for achieving physical perfection and spiritual mastery.</summary>
<keywords>Shakti-chalan, Kundalini, Root Chakra, Apana Vayu, Mudras, Siddha, Shiva Samhita, Yoga perfections, Animā, immortality</keywords>

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Goddess Parvati and the Lord Shiva, moving into Chapter 5, which focuses on the various distractions that prevent a practitioner from achieving success in Yoga.</meta>

# CHAPTER V.
## Now begins the Fifth Chapter. <note>original: "Atha pañcamaḥ paṭalaḥ"</note>

-> The Goddess said: Speak to me, O Lord, for the sake of those whose minds are fixed on the highest truth. <-
-> Tell me, my dear <term>Shankar</term>, what obstacles exist for people in this world? — 1. <-
<note>Shankar is a name for Shiva meaning "the bringer of happiness" or "the benevolent one."</note>

<term>Pârvati</term>—O Lord, O beloved <term>Shankar</term>! tell me, for the sake of those whose minds search after the supreme end, *the obstacles and the hindrances to <term>Yoga</term>.*
<note>Yoga here refers to the systematic practice of meditation and physical discipline intended to lead to spiritual liberation.</note>

-> The Lord said: Listen, O Goddess! I shall explain how obstacles are always present. <-
-> For humans seeking liberation, worldly enjoyment is the ultimate bond. — 2. <-

<insert>2.</insert> <term>Śiva</term>—Hear, O Goddess! I shall tell thee, all the obstacles that stand in the path of <term>Yoga</term>. For the attainment of emancipation, enjoyments (<term>bhoga</term>) are the greatest of all impediments.
<note>Emancipation (mukti) refers to the soul's release from the cycle of birth and death.</note>

-> <term>Bhoga</term> (enjoyment). <-

-> Now begins the description of obstacles to Yoga in the form of worldly enjoyments. <note>original: "atha bhogarūpayogavighnakathanam"</note> <-

-> Women, beds, seats, clothing, and riches are distractions. <-
-> Betel leaves, dainty foods, vehicles, kingdoms, power, and Greatness. <-
-> Gold, silver, as well as copper, gems, aloe wood, and cattle. <-
-> Scholarship, the <term>Vedas</term> and <term>Śâstras</term>, dancing, singing, and ornaments. <-
-> The flute, the lute, the drum, and riding on elephants or horses. <-
-> Wives and children, and worldly objects—these are proclaimed as obstacles. <-
-> These are the obstacles in the form of enjoyment; now hear those in the form of religious duty. — 3. <-

3. Women, beds, seats, dresses, and riches are obstacles to <term>Yoga</term>. Betels <note>a leaf chewed as a mild stimulant</note>, dainty dishes, carriages, kingdoms, lordliness and powers; gold, silver, as well as copper, gems, aloe wood, and kine <note>archaic term for cows</note>; learning the <term>Vedas</term> <note>the oldest scriptures of Hinduism</note> and the <term>Śâstras</term> <note>technical or specialized texts on law and discipline</note>; dancing, singing and ornaments; harp, flute and drum; riding on elephants and horses; wives and children, worldly enjoyments; all these are so many impediments. These are the obstacles which arise from <term>bhoga</term> (enjoyment). Hear now the impediments which arise from ritualistic religion.

-> <term>Dharma</term> (ritualism of Religion). <-

-> Now begins the description of obstacles to Yoga in the form of religious duties. <note>original: "atha dharmarūpayogavighnakathanam"</note> <-

-> Ritual bathing, methods of worship, fire sacrifices, and even the pursuit of liberation. <-
-> Vows, fasting, observances, silence, and the restraint of the senses. <-
-> The object of meditation, meditation itself, as well as mantras, charity, and seeking fame in all directions. <-
<note>The text suggests that even "good" religious activities can become distractions if they become ends in themselves rather than means to spiritual union.</note>

<summary>This page begins the fifth chapter with Parvati asking Shiva to identify the distractions that prevent success in Yoga. Shiva explains that both worldly pleasures (bhoga) and even strict religious rituals (dharma) can act as golden chains that bind the seeker.</summary>
<keywords>Yoga, Parvati, Shiva, Bhoga, Dharma, obstacles to meditation, worldly distractions, ritualism</keywords>

--- Page 59 ---
<meta>This page continues the classification of obstacles to spiritual success from the previous section. Here, the text shifts from general worldly distractions to more subtle "virtuous" distractions, specifically those arising from religious duties and the technical study of yoga.</meta>

### CHAPTER V. 4-7.

<note>original Sanskrit: वापीकूपतडागादिप्रासादारामकल्पना । यज्ञं चान्द्रायणं कृच्छ्रं तीर्थानि विविधानि च । दृश्यन्ते च इमे विघ्ना धर्मरूपेण संस्थिताः ॥ ४ ॥</note>

4. The following are the obstacles which <term>dharma</term> <note>Righteous duty or religious merit</note> interposes:— ablutions, worship of deities, observing the sacred days of the moon, fire sacrifice, hankering after <term>moksha</term> <note>Final liberation from the cycle of rebirth</note>, vows and penances, fasts, religious observances, silence, ascetic practices, contemplation and the object of contemplation, <term>mantras</term> <note>Sacred chants or invocations</note>, and alms-giving, world-wide fame, the excavating and endowing of tanks, wells, ponds, convents and groves; sacrifices, vows of starvation, <term>Chandrayana</term> <note>A lunar penance where food intake follows the phases of the moon</note>, and pilgrimages.

---

->*Knowledge as an Obstacle*<-
<note>original: Jñâna (Knowledge-obstacles)</note>

->**Now, the description of obstacles in the form of knowledge.**<-
<note>original: अथ ज्ञानरूपविघ्नकथनम् ।</note>

<note>original Sanskrit: यत्तु विघ्नं भवेज्ज्ञानं कथयामि वरानने । गोमुखं स्वासनं कृत्वा धौतिप्रक्षालनं च तत् । नाडीसञ्चारविज्ञानं प्रत्याहारनिरोधनम् । कुक्षिसञ्चालनं क्षिप्रं प्रवेश इन्द्रियाध्वना । नाडीकर्माणि कल्याणि भोजनं श्रयतां मम ॥ ५ ॥</note>

5. Now I shall describe, O <note>The text addresses the goddess Parvati as "Fair-faced one"</note>, the obstacles which arise from the pursuit of intellectual knowledge. Sitting in the <term>Gomukha</term> <note>The "cow-face" posture, a seated yoga pose</note> posture and practicing <term>Dhauti</term> <note>Internal cleansing techniques, such as washing the digestive tract</note> (a part of <term>Hatha Yoga</term> <note>A physical system of yoga focused on postures and breath</note>). Knowledge of the distribution of the <term>nadis</term> <note>The subtle channels through which vital energy flows in the body</note>, the learning of <term>pratyahara</term> <note>The practice of withdrawing the senses from external objects</note>, trying to awaken the <term>Kundalini</term> <note>The dormant spiritual energy located at the base of the spine</note> force by moving the belly quickly <note>A reference to Nauli or similar abdominal churning</note>, entering into the path of the <term>indriyas</term> <note>The sensory and motor organs</note>, and the technical knowledge of the action of the <term>nadis</term>; these are all obstacles. Now listen to the mistaken notions regarding diet, O Auspicious One.

<note>original Sanskrit: नवधातु रसं छिन्नु शुण्ठिकात्ताडयेत्पुनः । एककालं समाधिः स्याल्लिङ्गभूतमिदं शृणु ॥ ६ ॥</note>

6. It is a mistake to believe that <term>samadhi</term> <note>A state of deep meditative absorption or spiritual ecstasy</note> can be induced at once by drinking certain new chemical essences or by eating certain kinds of food. Now hear about the mistaken notions regarding the influence of company.

<note>original Sanskrit: सङ्गं गच्छ साधूनां संकोचं भज दुर्जनात् । प्रवेशनिर्गमे वायोगुर्लक्षं विलोकयेत् ॥ ७ ॥</note>

7. The idea that one must "keep the company of the virtuous and avoid that of the vicious" is actually a mistaken notion <note>In this high state of non-dual yoga, even the distinction between "good" and "bad" people is seen as a mental construct that can hinder progress</note>. So too is the obsession with measuring the heaviness and lightness of the inhaled and exhaled air.

<summary>This page lists the subtle "virtuous" obstacles to spiritual progress, arguing that even religious charity, pilgrimages, and the technical, physical aspects of Hatha Yoga can become distractions if they are treated as ends in themselves.</summary>
<keywords>Dharma, Knowledge obstacles, Hatha Yoga, Nadis, Kundalini, Samadhi, Asceticism, Pratyahara</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page continues the classification of spiritual obstacles and begins the systematic categorization of Yoga practitioners based on their capabilities, following the philosophical foundations laid out in previous sections of the Shiva Samhita.</meta>

# THE SHIVA SAMHITA (Page 54)

---

->Whether the Absolute is within the body, or is the maker of forms, or possesses a form, or is formless;<-
->Or if the Absolute is everything—the mind finds rest in these various states.<-
->Thus have I described to you the obstacles that exist in the guise of knowledge.<-

8. Whether one believes the Absolute <note>original: "Brahman"</note> is confined to the physical body, or is the creator of form, or possesses a specific form, or is entirely formless, or is the totality of all things—all these comforting intellectual doctrines are actually hurdles. Such concepts are impediments that take the shape of <term>Jnana</term> <note>original: "Jñaña"; here meaning intellectual or theoretical knowledge that acts as a distraction from direct experience</note>.

### *Four Kinds of Yoga.*

->Now follows the description of the four types of Yoga.<-

->(Mantra-Yoga, Hatha-Yoga, and Laya-Yoga is the third;<-
->The fourth is Raja-Yoga, which is free from all sense of duality.<-

9. Yoga is of four types: First, <term>Mantra-Yoga</term> <note>the path of sacred sound and chanting</note>; second, <term>Hatha-Yoga</term> <note>the path of physical discipline and breath control</note>; third, <term>Laya-Yoga</term> <note>the yoga of meditative absorption or dissolution</note>; and fourth, <term>Raja-Yoga</term> <note>the "Royal Yoga" of deep meditation</note>, which discards the perception of separation between the self and the divine.

### *Aspirants (Sadhakas).*

->The aspirant should be known as being of four types: the mild, the moderate, the ardent;<-
->And the most ardent, who is the best and capable of crossing the ocean of worldly existence.<-

10. Know that seekers <note>original: "sâdhaks"</note> are classified into four orders: the mild, the moderate, the ardent, and the most ardent—the latter being the most capable of crossing the "ocean" of worldly suffering and rebirth.

### *The Mild Aspirant entitled to Mantra-Yoga.*

->Now, the characteristics of the mild aspirant.<-

->One who lacks enthusiasm, is deeply deluded, sickly, and speaks ill of their teachers;<-
->Greedy, sinful in mind, a glutton, and overly dependent on women;<-
->Fickle, timid, diseased, lacking independence, and very cruel;<-
->One of poor character and low energy is known as a mild human being.<-
->Success for such a person comes after twelve years of great effort.<-
->The teacher should certainly recognize such a person as fit for Mantra-Yoga.<-

11. Men of small ambition, those who are easily confused, sickly, or critical of their instructors; those who are greedy, prone to sinful thoughts, over-eaters, or helplessly attached to their wives; the unstable, the fearful, the diseased, the dependent, and the harsh—those whose characters are poor and whose vitality is weak—know all these to be mild <term>sadhakas</term>. With great effort, such individuals may find success in twelve years; the teacher should recognize them as suited specifically for <term>Mantra-Yoga</term>.

### *The Moderate Aspirant entitled to Laya-Yoga.*

->One of even mind, endowed with forgiveness, desiring virtuous merit, and kind in speech;<-
->Who is moderate in all undertakings, is undoubtedly a "moderate" seeker.<-
->Knowing this, the teachers grant them the path of absorption for the sake of liberation.<-

12. A person of steady temperament, full of forgiveness, seeking to do good, and gentle in their speech; one who is balanced and moderate in all their actions is undoubtedly a moderate seeker. Recognizing these qualities, the teachers initiate them into <term>Laya-Yoga</term> <note>the Yoga of dissolution or mental absorption</note> to help them achieve liberation.

<summary>This page defines intellectual concepts as potential obstacles to true knowledge and categorizes spiritual aspirants into four levels—mild, moderate, ardent, and most ardent—assigning the mild and moderate seekers to Mantra-Yoga and Laya-Yoga respectively.</summary>

<keywords>Shiva Samhita, Brahman, Jnana, Mantra-Yoga, Hatha-Yoga, Laya-Yoga, Raja-Yoga, Sadhaka, types of aspirants, spiritual obstacles</keywords>

### CHAPTER V. 12-14.

12. Liberal-minded, merciful, desirous of virtue, sweet in their speech; who never go to extremes in any undertaking—these are the middling <note>In Sanskrit: *madhyama*</note>. These are to be initiated by the teacher in <term>Laya-Yoga</term> <note>A system of yoga focused on "dissolution" or total absorption of the mind into a state of meditative union.</note>.

->(*Ardent*) entitled to <term>Hatha Yoga</term><-

->**Now, the characteristics of the superior seeker.**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "अथ अधिमात्रसाधकलक्षणम्" (Atha adhimātra-sādhaka-lakṣaṇam)</note>

->Steady-minded, devoted to the practice of absorption, independent, full of vigor;<-
->High-minded, compassionate, forgiving, and also truthful;<-
->Brave, in the prime of life, faithful, a worshiper of the lotus-feet of the teacher;<-
->Devoted to the practice of yoga—such a person is known as superior.<-
->Success for this person comes in six years through the union of practice;<-
->To such a steady person, Hatha Yoga and its limbs are given.<-
<note>The lines above translate the Sanskrit verses 13.1 through 13.6 provided in the text.</note>

13. Steady-minded, knowing the <term>Laya-Yoga</term>, independent, full of energy, magnanimous, full of sympathy, forgiving, truthful, courageous, full of faith, worshippers of the lotus-feet of their <term>Gurus</term> <note>Spiritual teachers or masters.</note>, engaged always in the practice of Yoga,—know such men to be <term>adhimâtra</term> <note>Literally "above measure," referring to a superior or highly capable student.</note>. They obtain success in the practice of Yoga within six years, and ought to be initiated in <term>Haṭha-Yoga</term> <note>A foundational practice of yoga focusing on physical postures and breath control to purify the body and mind.</note> and its branches.

->(*The most ardent*) entitled to all *Yogas*<-

->**Now, the characteristics of the most superior seeker.**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "अथ अधिमात्रतमसाधकलक्षणम्" (Atha adhimātratama-sādhaka-lakṣaṇam)</note>

->Possessed of great vigor and enthusiasm, charming, and also heroic;<-
->Knowledgeable in the scriptures, habitual in practice, free from delusion, and untroubled;<-
->Endowed with the vigor of youth, moderate in eating, having conquered the senses;<-
->Fearless, pure, skillful, charitable, a refuge for all people;<-
->Competent, steady, wise, residing as they wish, forgiving;<-
->Good-natured, practicing righteousness, keeping their actions secret, speaking kindly;<-
->Full of faith in the scriptures, a worshiper of the gods and the teacher;<-
->Detached from the company of many people, free from great diseases;<-
->Knowing the superior vows, a practitioner of all yogas;<-
->Within three years, success comes to such a person, without a doubt;<-
->They are eligible for all forms of yoga; of this there is no need for further thought.<-
<note>The lines above translate the Sanskrit verses 14.1 through 14.12 provided in the text.</note>

14. Those who have the largest amount of energy, are enterprising, engaging, heroic, who know the <term>śastras</term> <note>The sacred scriptures or technical manuals of the tradition.</note>, and are persevering, *free from the effects of blind emotions*, and, not easily confused, who are in the prime of their youth, moderate in their diet, rulers of their senses, fearless, clean, skilful, charitable, a help to all; competent, firm, talented, contented, forgiving, good-natured, religious, who keep their endeavours secret, of sweet speech, peaceful, who have faith in scriptures and are worshippers <note>This description identifies the highest grade of practitioner, the *adhimātratama*, who is considered ready for the most advanced spiritual practices and can achieve success in as little as three years.</note>

<summary>This page defines the characteristics of the three higher grades of Yoga students—middling, superior, and most superior—detailing their personality traits, their eligibility for different types of Yoga (Laya, Hatha, or all), and the time required for them to achieve success.</summary>
<keywords>Laya-Yoga, Adhimâtra, Haṭha-Yoga, śastras, Guru, Adhimâtratama, seeker levels, Yoga success, student characteristics</keywords>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the qualifications required for a Yoga practitioner to achieve success and introduces the specific practice of shadow gazing.</meta>

...of God and Guru, who are averse to wasting their time in social idle-talk, and are free from any serious illness, who are acquainted with the duties of the <term>highly qualified student</term> <note>original: "adhimâtra"; referring to a student of the highest intensity and spiritual readiness</note>, and are the practitioners of every kind of Yoga—undoubtedly, they obtain success in three years; they are entitled to be initiated into all kinds of Yoga without any hesitation.

### *Invocation of the shadow (pratikopâsana).*

> **Now begins the instruction on shadow invocation.** <note>original: "Atha pratīkopāsanam"</note>
> **The invocation of the shadow should be performed as it grants results both seen and unseen.**
> **By simply seeing it, one becomes pure; of this there is no doubt.** || 15 ||

15. The invocation of the <term>Shadow-Image</term> <note>original: "Pratika"; a symbol or reflection used as a point of focus</note> gives to the devotee rewards in both the physical and spiritual worlds; undoubtedly, by its very sight, a person becomes pure.

> **In the intense heat of the sun, looking at the shadow of one's own body with eyes wide open.**
> **When one then looks into the sky, they see their own shadow-form in the courtyard of the heavens at that very moment.** || 16 ||

16. In a clear sun-lit sky, behold with a steady gaze the reflection of your own divine form <note>specifically by looking at the neck of your shadow on the ground first</note>; whenever this reflection is seen even for a single second in the sky, you behold the Divine at once in the heavens.

> **He who daily perceives his own shadow-form in the courtyard of the sky.**
> **His lifespan will be increased and he shall never suffer an accidental death.** || 17 ||

17. He who daily sees his shadow in the sky will have his years of life increased and will never die an untimely or accidental death.

> **When he sees the shadow-form complete in every limb in the courtyard of the sky.**
> **Then he obtains victory; and having conquered the vital breath, he may travel anywhere.** || 18 ||

18. When the shadow is seen fully reflected in the field of the sky, then the practitioner obtains victory; and conquering the <term>vital breath</term> <note>original: "vâyu"; the life-force or internal winds</note>, he gains the power to go everywhere.

### *How to invoke.*

At the time of the rising sun, or by the light of the moon, let the practitioner steadily fix his gaze on the neck of the shadow he throws on the ground; then, after some time, let him look up into the sky; if he sees a full grey shadow appearing in the sky, it is a highly auspicious sign.

> **He who always practices this and honors the Supreme Self.**
> **Becomes a man of absolute bliss through the grace of his own shadow-form.** || 19 ||

19. He who always practices this and realizes the <term>Supreme Soul</term> <note>original: "Paramâtmâ"</note>, becomes entirely filled with bliss through the grace of his shadow-form.

<summary>This page details the practice of Pratikopasana, or shadow-gazing, a technique used by advanced yogis to achieve spiritual purity, longevity, and the ability to travel at will by mastering the vital airs.</summary>
<keywords>Siva Samhita, shadow invocation, Pratikopasana, vayu, Paramatma, yoga mastery, longevity, spiritual reflection</keywords>

--- Page 63 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for yogic practices in Chapter V, transitioning from the meditation on the shadow (Pratikopasana) to the techniques of Raja Yoga (Royal Yoga).</meta>

<page-num>57</page-num>
<header>CHAPTER V. 20-26.</header>

->At the time of starting a journey, at a wedding, during any auspicious work, or when facing a crisis, one should perform the meditation on the reflection. This practice destroys sins and increases merit. <note>original: "यात्राकाले विवाहे च शुभे कर्मणि सङ्कटे । पापक्षये पुण्यवृद्धौ प्रतीकोपासनञ्चरेत् ॥ २० ॥" — The Sanskrit text refers to Pratikopasana, which literally means "meditation on a symbol," but in this context refers to a specific technique of gazing at one's own shadow.</note> ॥ 20 ॥<-

20. At the time of commencing travel, marriage, or auspicious work, or when in trouble, it is of great use. This invocation of the shadow destroys sins and increases virtue.

->By practicing this continually, the practitioner eventually sees the shadow-form within their own heart; then the <term>Yogi</term><note>A practitioner of Yoga.</note> of steady mind attains liberation. <note>original: "निरन्तरकृताभ्यासादन्तरे पश्यति ध्रुवम् । तदा मुक्तिमवाप्नोति योगी नियतमानसः ॥ २१ ॥"</note> ॥ 21 ॥<-

21. By practising it always, he begins at last to see it in his heart, and the persevering <term>Yogi</term> gets liberation.

### *Râj Yoga* <note>The "Royal Yoga," often considered the highest path of meditation and breath control.</note>

->The <term>Yogi</term> should firmly close both ears with the thumbs, both eyes with the index fingers, the nostrils with the middle fingers, and the mouth with the remaining fingers. By thus completely restraining the breath, the practitioner sees the Self in the form of light. <note>original: "अंगुष्ठाभ्यामुभे श्रोत्रे... तदा लक्षणमात्मानं ज्योतिरूपं स पश्यति ॥ २२ ॥" — This describes the Shanmukhi Mudra, or the "gesture of the six gates."</note> ॥ 22 ॥<-

22. Let him close the ears with his thumbs, the eyes with index fingers, the nostril with the middle fingers, and with the remaining four fingers let him press together the upper and lower lips. The <term>Yogi</term>, by having thus firmly confined the air, sees his soul in the shape of light.

->Whoever sees this light, even for a single moment without distraction, is freed from all sins and reaches the highest goal. <note>original: "तत्त्वे जो दृश्यते येन क्षणमात्रं निराकुलम् । सर्वपापविनिर्मुक्तः स याति परमां गतिम् ॥ २३ ॥"</note> ॥ 23 ॥<-

23. When one sees, without obstruction, this light for even a moment, becoming free from sin, he reaches the highest end.

->The <term>Yogi</term> who is free from sin and practices this constantly forgets the physical, subtle, and causal bodies, and becomes one with that soul. <note>original: "निरन्तरकृताभ्यासाद्योगी विगतकल्मषः । सर्वदेहादि विस्मृत्य तदभिन्नः स्वयं गतः ॥ २४ ॥" — In Yogic philosophy, a person consists of three "bodies": the gross physical body, the subtle energetic body, and the causal body of seeds of action.</note> ॥ 24 ॥<-

24. The <term>Yogi</term>, free from sin, and practising this continually, forgets his physical, subtle and causal bodies, and becomes one with that soul.

->The person who practices this in secret always becomes absorbed in the <term>Brahman</term><note>The ultimate, infinite reality or Divine Ground of all being.</note>, even if they had previously been devoted to sinful deeds. <note>original: "यः करोति सदाभ्यासं गुप्ताचारेण मानवः । स वै ब्रह्मविलीनः स्यात्यापकर्मरतो यदि ॥ २५ ॥"</note> ॥ 25 ॥<-

25. He who practises this in secrecy, is absorbed in the <term>Brahman</term>, though he had been engaged in sinful works.

->This practice should be kept secret with great care; it produces immediate conviction and grants liberation to all in this world. It is my most beloved <term>Yoga</term>. Through gradual practice, the mystic sounds begin to manifest. <note>original: "गोपनीयः प्रयत्नेन... नादः संज्ञायते तस्य क्रमेणाभ्यासतःच वै ॥ २६ ॥" — The "nadas" are internal sounds, like bells or flutes, heard during deep meditation.</note> ॥ 26 ॥<-

26. This should be kept secret; it at once produces conviction ; it gives <term>nirvâna</term><note>The state of final liberation from the cycle of birth and death.</note> to mankind. This is my most beloved <term>Yoga</term>. From practising this gradually, the <term>Yogi</term> begins to hear the mystic sounds (<term>nâdas</term>).

<summary>This page details the transition from meditation on the shadow to the practices of Raja Yoga, specifically describing a breathing and sensory withdrawal technique used to perceive the soul as light. It emphasizes the spiritual benefits of these practices, including the destruction of sin, union with the Supreme Reality (Brahman), and the hearing of internal mystic sounds.</summary>
<keywords>Raja Yoga, Shanmukhi Mudra, Brahman, Nirvana, Nadas, liberation, meditation on light, shadow meditation, Yoga philosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions on advanced meditative absorption (Laya) and the internal sounds heard during the practice of Yoga.</meta>

SIVA SAMHITA.

### *The Unstruck Sounds.* <note>original: "Anhad Sounds". These are the 'Anahata' sounds, which are mystical vibrations heard within the body during deep meditation that are not caused by any physical striking.</note>

->The first sound is like the hum of a honey-intoxicated bee, then that of a flute, and then a harp. After practicing in this way, the darkness of the world is destroyed.<-
->Next, a sound like a ringing bell is heard, and then a sound like a roar of thunder. When the mind is fixed on this sound and remains without fear,<-
->then, O my beloved, that state of absorption is born within him.<-

27. The first sound is like the hum of the honey-intoxicated bee, next that of a flute, then of a harp; after this, by the gradual practice of <term>Yoga</term> <note>a physical, mental, and spiritual discipline aimed at liberating the soul</note>, the destroyer of the darkness of the world, he hears the sounds of ringing bells; then sounds like the roar of thunder. When one fixes his full attention on this sound, being free from fear, he gets absorption, O my beloved!

->When the mind of the practitioner takes great delight in that sound, forgetting all things outside, it becomes still and peaceful along with the sound.<-

28. When the mind of the <term>Yogi</term> <note>a practitioner of Yoga</note> is exceedingly engaged in this sound, he forgets all external things, and is absorbed in this sound.

->Through the practice of this yoga, having fully conquered the three qualities and abandoning all worldly undertakings, one dissolves into the ether of consciousness.<-

29. By this practice of <term>Yoga</term> he conquers all the three <term>Gunas</term> <note>original: "qualities". These are Sattva (purity), Rajas (activity), and Tamas (inertia)</note>; and being free from all states, he is absorbed in <term>chidâkâs</term> <note>original: "the ether of intelligence". This refers to the vast, infinite space of pure consciousness.</note>

### *A Secret.*

->There is no posture like the 'Perfect Pose,' no power like 'Breath Retention.' There is no seal like the 'Sky-Walker,' and no absorption like that of the 'Inner Sound.'<-

30 There is no posture like that of <term>Siddhâsana</term> <note>the 'Perfect Pose,' a seated posture used for meditation</note>, no power like that of <term>Kumbha</term> <note>Kumbhaka, or the practice of holding the breath</note>, no <term>Mudrâ</term> <note>a symbolic gesture or 'seal' used to control energy</note> like the <term>Khechari</term> <note>a practice where the tongue is used to seal the energy channels, literally meaning 'one who moves through the sky'</note>, and no absorption like that of <term>nâda</term> <note>the mystic inner sound</note>.

->Now I shall tell you of the experience of the liberated soul, my dear. By knowing this, even a practitioner burdened by sins attains liberation.<-

31. Now I shall describe to thee, O dear, the foretaste of salvation, knowing which even the sinful aspirant may obtain salvation.

->Having worshipped the Lord properly and performed the highest yoga, the wise one, having pleased his teacher and becoming steady, should take up this practice.<-

32. Having adored the Lord God properly, and having completely performed the best of the <term>Yogas</term>, and being in a calm and steady state and posture, let the wise <term>Yogi</term> initiate himself into this <term>Yoga</term> by pleasing his <term>Guru</term> <note>a spiritual teacher or guide</note>.

<summary>This page describes the sequence of internal sounds (Nada) heard during deep meditation, progressing from bee-hums to thunder, leading to spiritual absorption. It also highlights the supreme importance of specific yogic techniques like Siddhasana and Khechari Mudra as the most effective paths to liberation.</summary>
<keywords>Anahata sounds, internal meditation, Siddhasana, Khechari Mudra, Chidakasha, spiritual liberation, Yoga practice, Nada Yoga, Gunas, Guru</keywords>
