<meta>This page continues the instructions from Chapter V regarding the practical and ritual requirements for the advanced practitioner of Yoga.</meta>

### CHAPTER V. 33-39.

->**Giving all possessions, such as cattle, to the <term>Guru</term> who knows <term>Yoga</term>, and having satisfied him with great effort, the wise receive this initiation. ॥ 33 ॥**<-
<note>original: "जीवादि सकलं वस्तुं दत्त्वा योगविदं गुरुम् । सन्तोष्यातिप्रयत्नेन योगोऽयं गृह्यते बुधैः ॥ ३३ ॥"</note>

33. Having given all his cattle and property to the <term>Guru</term> <note>A spiritual teacher or guide.</note> who knows <term>Yoga</term>, and having satisfied him with great care, let the wise man receive this initiation.

->**Having pleased the <term>Brahmans</term> with various auspicious gifts, let the wise man, becoming pure in my temple, receive this auspicious teaching. ॥ 34 ॥**<-
<note>original: "विप्रान्संतोष्य मेधावी नानामंगलसंयुतः । ममालये शुचिर्भूत्वा प्रगृह्णीयाच्छुभात्मकम् ॥ ३४ ॥"</note>

34. Having pleased the <term>Brahmans</term> <note>Members of the priestly and scholarly class.</note> (and priest), by giving them all kinds of good things, let the wise man receive this auspicious <term>Yoga</term> in my house (*i.e.*, the temple of Shiva) with purity of heart.

->**Having renounced by this method the physical body and its past attachments, the <term>Yogi</term>, having attained a divine body, should receive what is to be told. ॥ 35 ॥**<-
<note>original: "संन्यस्यानेन विधिना प्राक्तनं विग्रहादिकम् । भूत्वा दिव्यवपुर्योगी गृह्णीयाद्धक्ष्यमाणकम् ॥ ३५ ॥"</note>

35. Having renounced by the above methods all his previous bodies (the results of his past <term>karma</term> <note>The law of action and consequence; the spiritual "residue" of previous deeds.</note>), and being in his spiritual (or luminous) body, let the <term>Yogi</term> receive this highest <term>Yoga</term>.

->**The <term>Yogi</term>, sitting in the <term>Padmasana</term> posture and avoiding the company of men, should block the two <term>Vijnana Nadis</term> with his fingers. ॥ 36 ॥**<-
<note>original: "पद्मासनस्थितो योगी जनसंगविवर्जितः । विज्ञाननाडीद्वितयमंगुलीभ्यां निरोधयेत् ॥ ३६ ॥"</note>

36. Sitting in the <term>padmâsana</term> <note>The "Lotus Position," a cross-legged sitting posture used for meditation.</note> posture, renouncing the society of men, let the <term>Yogi</term> press the two <term>vijnâna nâdis</term> <note>Literally "vessels of consciousness"; historically identified by some as the carotid arteries or specific energy channels in the head.</note> (the vessels of consciousness, perhaps coronal arteries) with his two fingers.

->**Upon attaining success, the stainless one, who is the very form of happiness, appears. Effort should be made in this practice so that success is truly achieved. ॥ 37 ॥**<-
<note>original: "सिद्धेस्तदाविर्भवति सुखरूपी निरञ्जनः । तस्मिन्परिश्रमः कार्यो येन सिद्धो भवेत्खलु ॥ ३७ ॥"</note>

37. By obtaining success in this, he becomes all happiness and unstained; therefore, let him endeavour with all his might, in order to ensure success.

->**He who practices this always finds that success is not far off; he also attains <term>vayu-siddhi</term> gradually, without a doubt. ॥ 38 ॥**<-
<note>original: "यः करोति सदाभ्यासं तस्य सिद्धिर्न दूरतः । वायुसिद्धिर्भवेत्तस्य क्रमादेव न संशयः ॥ ३८ ॥"</note>

38. He who practises this always, obtains success within a short time; he gets also <term>vâyu-siddhi</term> <note>The perfection or mastery over the vital breaths.</note> in course of time.

->**The <term>Yogi</term> who performs this even once certainly destroys a flood of sins; undoubtedly, the <term>vayus</term> enter his middle channel. ॥ 39 ॥**<-
<note>original: "सकृद्यः कुरुते योगी पापौघं नाशयेद्ध्रुवम् । तस्य स्यान्मध्यमे वायोः प्रवेशो नात्र संशयः ॥ ३९ ॥"</note>

39. The <term>Yogi</term>, who does it even once, verily destroys all sins; and undoubtedly in him the <term>vâyus</term> <note>The vital life-forces or internal "airs."</note> enter the middle channel <note>The 'Sushumna' or central energy channel of the spine.</note>.

->**He who is devoted to this practice is a <term>Yogi</term> worshipped by the gods. Having obtained powers like <term>Anima</term>, he wanders through the three worlds. ॥ 40 ॥**<-
<note>original: "एतदभ्यासशीलो यः स योगी देवपूजितः । अणिमादिगुणाँल्लब्ध्वा विचरेद्भुवनत्रये ॥ ४० ॥"</note>
<note>The term 'Anima' refers to the siddhi or supernatural power of becoming as small as an atom.</note>

<summary>This page details the ritual preparations and physical techniques for a specific yogic initiation, emphasizing the need to please the Guru and Brahmans, and describing a meditation technique involving the restriction of the "vessels of consciousness."</summary>
<keywords>Guru, Yoga, Karma, Brahmans, Padmasana, Vijnana Nadis, Vayu-siddhi, Sushumna, spiritual purification, Anima</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions on yogic practice from the Siva Samhita, focusing on the fruits of practice and specific techniques for concentration (Dharana).</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA.

40. The Yogi who practices this with perseverance is worshipped even by gods; he receives the psychic powers of <term>animâ</term> <note>the ability to become as small as an atom</note>, <term>laghimâ</term> <note>the ability to become weightless</note> etc., and can go everywhere, throughout the three worlds, at pleasure.

->**As one masters the practice of the vital breath, so does their physical form become.**<-
->**The wise practitioner, established in the Self, enjoys great freedom in this world. || 41 ||**<-

41. According to the strength of one's practice in commanding the <term>vâyu</term> <note>the vital air or breath</note>, he gets command over his body; the wise, remaining in the spirit, enjoys the world in the present body.

->**This Yoga is a supreme secret, not to be given to just anyone;**<-
->**it is revealed only to those who are truly proven to have the right qualities. || 42 ||**<-

42. This Yoga is a great secret, and not to be given to every body; it might be revealed to him only, in whom all the qualifications of a Yogi are perceived.

### *Various kinds of <term>Dhâranâ</term> <note>Concentration or focused attention</note>.*

->**Let the Yogi sit in the Lotus Posture and meditate on the hollow of the throat;**<-
->**by placing the tongue at the base of the palate, hunger and thirst disappear. || 43 ||**<-

43. Let the Yogi seat himself in the <term>Padmâsana</term> <note>the Lotus Posture</note>, and fix his attention on the cavity of the throat, let him place his tongue at the base of the palate; by this he will extinguish hunger and thirst.

->**Below the cavity of the throat is the beautiful "Tortoise Channel."**<-
->**By fixing the mind there, the Yogi attains great steadiness of thought. || 44 ||**<-

44. Below the cavity of the throat, there is a beautiful <term>nâḍi</term> <note>an internal energy channel</note> called <term>kurma</term> <note>literally "tortoise," representing stability</note>; when the Yogi fixes his attention on it, he acquires great concentration of the thinking principle (<term>chitta</term>).

->**When one meditates on the opening of the Eye of Rudra in the skull,**<-
->**then a brilliant light appears, shining like a mass of lightning.**<-
->**By merely contemplating this, all sins are destroyed;**<-
->**even a person of wicked deeds attains the highest state. || 45 ||**<-

45. When the Yogi constantly thinks that he has got a third eye—the eye of Shiva—in the middle of his forehead, he then perceives a fire brilliant like lightning. By contemplating on this light, all sins are destroyed, and even the most wicked person obtains the highest end.

->**When the wise practitioner meditates on this day and night,**<-
->**the sight and conversation of the perfected masters will surely be theirs. || 46 ||**<-

46. If the experienced Yogi thinks of this light day and night, he sees the <term>Siddhas</term> <note>adepts or perfected beings who have attained spiritual liberation</note>, and can certainly converse with them.

### CHAPTER V. 47–52.

->Whether standing, walking, sleeping, or eating, one should meditate on the <term>Shunya</term> (the Great Void or emptiness) day and night.<-
->Then the practitioner, becoming like the ether, merges into the <term>Chid-akasha</term> (the space of pure consciousness). 47.<-

48. The <term>Yogi</term> (practitioner of yoga), desiring success, should always obtain this knowledge; by habitual exercise, he becomes equal to me <note>In this text, the speaker is the god Shiva, the primordial teacher of Yoga.</note>; through the force of this knowledge, he becomes beloved by all.

49. Having conquered all the elements, and being free from all hopes and worldly attachments, when the practitioner, sitting in the <term>Padmasana</term> (Lotus posture), fixes his gaze on the tip of the nose, his mind becomes still and he obtains the spiritual power called <term>Khechari</term> <note>Literally "sky-walking," a state of consciousness or physical power associated with moving through the subtle heavens.</note>. 

50. The great practitioner beholds a light, pure as the holy mountain <note>original: "Kailas," the crystalline mountain home of Shiva.</note>, and through the force of his exercise in it, he becomes the lord and guardian of that light.

51. Stretching himself out on the ground, let him meditate continuously; the wise practitioner does this to instantly destroy all weariness and fatigue. By meditating on the back part of the head, he becomes the conqueror of death. (We have described before the effect of fixing one's attention on the space between the two eyebrows, so it need not be repeated here).

->The essence of the four kinds of food is divided into three parts.<-
->The most subtle part nourishes the <term>linga deha</term> (the subtle or astral body).<-
->The middle part enters and nourishes the physical body, which is composed of seven <term>dhatus</term> (tissues). 52.<-
<gloss>The four kinds of food refer to those that are chewed, swallowed, licked, and sucked.</gloss>

<summary>This page describes advanced meditation techniques involving the "Great Void" and physical focal points to achieve spiritual absorption, mastery over fatigue, and the nourishment of the subtle body.</summary>
<keywords>Shunya, Chid-akasha, Yoga, Padmasana, Khechari, Kailash, meditation, astral body, seven tissues, Shiva</keywords>

--- Page 68 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the body's internal energetic structure, moving from the digestion of food to the location of the primary energy centers (Chakras).</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA.

52. Of the four kinds of food (that which is chewed, sucked, licked, and drunk) which a person consumes, the chyle fluid <note>The milky fluid drained from food during digestion.</note> is divided into three parts. The best part (the finest extract) nourishes the <term>linga sharira</term> <note>The "subtle body" or "subtle form"—the energetic blueprint of a human that exists behind the physical flesh.</note> or subtle body, which is the seat of force. The second or middle part nourishes the gross physical body, which is composed of seven <term>dhâtus</term> <note>The seven fundamental tissues: plasma, blood, muscle, fat, bone, marrow, and reproductive fluid.</note>.

->It passes out as excrement and urine as the third part, exiting the body.<-
->The first two parts enter the channels mentioned; all of them<-
->nourish the body and the vital air from the soles of the feet to the head. || 53 ||<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "yāti viṇmūtrarūpeṇa tṛtīyaḥ saptato bahiḥ || ādyabhāgadvayaṃ nāḍyaḥ proktāstāḥ sakalā api | poṣayanti vapurvāyumāpādatalamastakam || 53 ||"</note>

53. The third or most inferior part leaves the body as waste. The first two essences of food are found within the <term>nâdis</term> <note>Energetic channels or conduits, similar to the concept of meridians in acupuncture.</note>, and as they are carried through them, they provide nourishment to the entire body from head to foot.

->When the vital air moves through all these channels,<-
->only then does the essence of food circulate throughout the body with balance. || 54 ||<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "nāḍībhirābhiḥ sarvābhirvāyuḥ sañcarate yadā | tadaivānna raso dehe sāmyeneha pravartate || 54 ||"</note>

54. When the <term>vâyu</term> <note>Literally "wind" or "air," referring here to the vital nerve force or internal breath.</note> moves through all the <term>nâdis</term>, then—facilitated by this <term>vâyu</term>—the fluids of the body gain extraordinary force and energy.

->Among those, there are fourteen principal channels in this process;<-
->they may be weak or strong, and they serve as the conduits for the movement of the vital breath. || 55 ||<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "caturdaśānāṃ tatre ha vyāpāre mukhyabhāgataḥ | tā anupratvahīnāśca prāṇasañcāranāḍikāḥ || 55 ||"</note>

55. The most important of these <term>nâdis</term> are fourteen in number, distributed throughout different parts of the body and performing various functions. They vary in strength, and the <term>prâna</term> <note>The fundamental life-force or vitality.</note> flows through them.

### *The six Chakras.* <note>The "wheels" or centers of spiritual energy located along the spine.</note>

### *Mulâdhâr Chakra.* <note>The "Root Support" center, located at the base of the spine.</note>

->Two fingers above the rectum and one finger below the genitals,<-
->there is a bulbous root that is four fingers in width. || 56 ||<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "gudādvyangulatascordhvaṃ meḍhrekāngulastvadhaḥ | evañcāsti samaṃ kandaṃ samatāccaturaṅgulam || 56 ||" Note: The OCR translation says "two fingers" below the genitals, but the Sanskrit 'ekāngulas' translates to 'one finger.'</note>

56. Located two fingers above the rectum and one finger below the <term>linga</term> <note>Here referring to the male reproductive organ.</note>, measuring four fingers in width, is a space shaped like a bulbous root.

->The "source" faces toward the back, situated between the rectum and the genitals;<-
->it is known as the bulbous root, and there the Coiled One always dwells.<-
->She surrounds all the channels in three and a half coils;<-
->placing her tail in her mouth, she rests at the opening of the central channel. || 57 ||<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "paścimābhimukhī: yonirg dumeḍhrāntarālagā | tatra kandaṃ samākhyātaṃ tatrāस्ति kuṇḍalī sadā || saṃveṣṭya sakalā nāḍīḥ sārddha trikuṭalākṛtīḥ | mukhe niveśya sā pucchaṃ suṣumṇāvivare sthitā || 57 ||"</note>

57. Between this space is the <term>yoni</term> <note>Literally "womb" or "source"; here it refers to the triangular space of the root center.</note> facing toward the back; that space is called the root. There dwells the goddess <term>Kundalini</term> <note>The latent spiritual energy, often depicted as a sleeping serpent.</note>. She surrounds all the <term>nâdis</term> and is coiled three and a half times. Holding her tail in her own mouth, she rests at the opening of the <term>Sushumnâ</term> <note>The most important energetic channel, running through the center of the spinal column.</note>.

<summary>This page describes how the body processes food into subtle and physical energies and introduces the anatomy of the Root Chakra (Muladhara), where the Kundalini energy resides.</summary>
<keywords>digestion, chyle, linga sharira, dhatus, nadis, vayu, prana, Muladhara Chakra, Kundalini, Sushumna</keywords>

# CHAPTER V. Verses 58-63.

-> She sleeps there like a serpent, and is luminous by her own light. <-
-> Like a serpent she lives between the joints; she is the goddess of speech, and is called the seed. ॥ 58 ॥ <-

58. She <note>The text refers to Kundalini, usually personified as a goddess.</note> sleeps there like a serpent, and is luminous by her own light. Like a serpent she lives between the joints; she is the goddess of speech, and is called the <term>seed</term> <note>original: vija; the foundational vibration or "seed-syllable" of power</note>.

-> Full of energy, and like burning gold, know this <term>Kundalini</term> to be the power of Vishnu; <-
-> she is the mother of the three qualities—purity, passion, and darkness. ॥ 59 ॥ <-

59. Full of energy, and like burning gold, know this <term>Kundalini</term> <note>The "Coiled One," representing the dormant spiritual energy at the base of the spine.</note> to be the <term>power</term> <note>original: shakti</note> of Vishnu; she is the mother of the three qualities—<term>purity</term> <note>original: sattwa; the quality of light, harmony, and goodness</note>, <term>passion</term> <note>original: rajas; the quality of movement, energy, and attachment</note>, and <term>darkness</term> <note>original: tamas; the quality of inertia, ignorance, and heaviness</note>.

-> There, beautiful like the Bandhuk flower, is placed the seed of love; <-
-> it is brilliant like burnished gold, and is described in Yoga as eternal. ॥ 60 ॥ <-

60. There, beautiful like the <term>Bandhuk flower</term> <note>A bright red flower (Pentapetes phoenicea) often associated with beauty and spiritual passion.</note>, is placed the seed of love <note>original: क्लीँ (Klīm), the seed-syllable associated with attraction and desire.</note>; it is brilliant like burnished gold, and is described in Yoga as eternal.

-> The central channel also embraces it, and the beautiful seed is there; <-
-> there it rests shining brilliantly like the autumnal moon, <-
-> with the luminosity of millions of suns, and the coolness of millions of moons. <-
-> The goddess <term>Tripura Bhairavi</term> has these three taken together, <-
-> and collectively she is called the seed. It is also called the great energy. ॥ 61 ॥ <-

61. The <term>central channel</term> <note>original: Sushumnâ; the primary energy pathway running up the spine.</note> also embraces it, and the beautiful seed is there; there it rests shining brilliantly like the autumnal moon, with the luminosity of millions of suns, and the coolness of millions of moons. The goddess <term>Tripura Bhairavi</term> <note>A fierce goddess representing the terrifying aspect of the divine feminine who rules over the three worlds.</note> has these three <note>fire, sun, and moon</note> taken together, and collectively she is called the <term>seed</term>. It is also called the great energy.

-> It is endowed with the powers of action and sensation, and circulates throughout the body. <-
-> It is subtle, and has a flame of fire; sometimes it rises up, and at other times it falls down into the water. <-
-> This is the great energy which rests in the perinaeum, and is called the self-born sign. ॥ 62 ॥ <-

62. It <note>the seed-energy</note> is endowed with the powers of action (motion) and sensation, and circulates throughout the body. It is subtle, and has a flame of fire; sometimes it rises up, and at other times it falls down into the water. This is the great energy which rests in the <term>perinaeum</term> <note>original: yonistha; the area at the base of the spine</note>, and is called the <term>self-born sign</term> <note>original: swayambhu-linga; a symbolic representation of eternal, unmanifested consciousness.</note>.

-> This is indeed the foundation-lotus, which has the perinaeum at its root. <-
-> It has four petals, marked with the letters Va, Sha, Sha, and Sa. ॥ 63 ॥ <-

63. All this is called the <term>foundation-lotus</term> <note>original: adhâr-padma, also known as the Muladhara or root chakra.</note>, and the four petals of it are designated by the letters <term>v</term> <note>original: व</note>, <term>ś</term> <note>original: श</note>, <term>ṣ</term> <note>original: ष</note>, and <term>s</term> <note>original: स</note>.

It explains the visual and symbolic attributes of this center, including its association with the goddess Tripura Bhairavi and the four-petaled lotus.</summary>

<page-num>64</page-num>

# SIVA SAMHITA

->The golden region called <term>Kula</term> <note>literally "family" or "lineage," here referring to the base of the spine</note> is associated with the <term>Swayambhu-linga</term> <note>the "self-existent" sign of Shiva</note>.<-
->Where the adept <term>Dviranda</term> resides and the goddess <term>Dakini</term> presides.<-
->In the center of that region is the <term>Yoni</term> <note>the source or womb</note> where <term>Kundalini</term> <note>the dormant spiritual energy often visualized as a coiled serpent</note> stays.<-
->Above it pulses a brilliant light, known as the <term>Kama-bija</term> <note>the "seed-syllable of desire"</note>.<-
->The wise practitioner who always meditates on this <term>Muladhara</term> <note>the root-support chakra</note><-
->attains the <term>Darduri-siddhi</term> <note>the "frog-jump" power</note>, eventually leaving the ground by degrees.<-

64. Near this **Swayambhu-linga** is a golden region called **Kula**; its presiding adept is called **Dviranda**, and its presiding goddess is called **Dakini**. In the center of that lotus is the **Yoni** where resides the **Kundalini**; the circulating bright energy above that is called **kama-vija** (the seed of love). The wise man who always contemplates on this **Muladhara** obtains **Darduri-siddhi** (the power to jump like a frog); and by degrees, he can altogether leave the ground (that is, rise into the air).

->The body’s brilliance is increased, the digestive fire is strengthened,<-
->and health, cleverness, and all-knowing wisdom are produced.<-

65. The brilliancy of the body is increased, the gastric fire <note>the "digestive fire" believed in Ayurvedic medicine to govern metabolism</note> becomes powerful, and freedom from disease, cleverness, and omniscience ensue.

->He knows the past, the present, and the future, along with their causes;<-
->even the unheard-of sciences, with their mysteries, become certain to him.<-

66. He knows what has been, what is happening, and what is to be, together with their causes; he masters the unheard-of sciences together with their mysteries.

->On his tongue, the goddess <term>Saraswati</term> <note>the Hindu goddess of knowledge, music, and the arts</note> dances constantly;<-
->he attains success in sacred chants through repetition alone, without a doubt.<-

67. On his tongue always dances the goddess of learning; he obtains **mantra-siddhi** (success in the use of sacred sounds) through constant repetition only.

->It destroys the flood of old age, death, and suffering—so says the Guru.<-
->The practitioner of breath control should always perform this meditation;<-
->by this contemplation alone, the great practitioner is freed from all sins.<-

68. This is the dictum of the Guru: "It destroys old age, death, and troubles innumerable." The practitioner of **pranayama** <note>the yogic practice of breath control</note> ought always to meditate upon it; by its very contemplation, the great **Yogi** <note>an advanced practitioner of yoga</note> is freed from all sins.

->When the Yogi meditates on the <term>Swayambhu-linga</term> in the root-lotus,<-
->at that very moment, the mass of his sins is certainly destroyed.<-

69. <note>The Sanskrit text here completes the thought that meditation on the root lotus instantly purifies the practitioner.</note>

<summary>This page describes the mystical components of the Muladhara (root chakra), including the Kundalini energy and the Swayambhu-linga. It details the supernatural benefits of meditating on this center, such as levitation, omniscience, and freedom from disease and sin.</summary>

# CHAPTER V. 69-75

<meta>This page continues the instructions on the Muladhara (root) chakra and introduces the Swadhishthana (pelvic) chakra. The header indicates the chapter and verse range. Handwritten archival number 33374 is noted at the top right, and the printer's signature '9' is at the bottom left.</meta>

69. When the Yogi contemplates this <term>Mulâdhâr</term> <note>The root energy center at the base of the spine.</note> lotus—the <term>Swayam-bhu-linga</term> <note>The "self-existent" symbol of divine consciousness, often depicted as a serpent coiled around a pillar.</note>—then, undoubtedly, at that very moment, all his sins are destroyed.

->Whatever the mind desires, he shall obtain that fruit. Through constant practice, he sees the Giver of Liberation. He who is the best, both within and without, is to be worshipped with great effort. In my opinion, there is nothing greater than Him.<- <note>original Sanskrit: "yaṃ yaṃ kāmayate citte..."</note>

70. Whatever the mind desires, he gets; by habitual exercise he sees him, who gives salvation, who is the best both in and out, and who is to be worshipped with great care. Better than Him, I know none.

->He who, leaving the Shiva residing within the self, worships that which is outside, is like one who throws away the food in his hand and wanders about in hopes of life.<- <note>original Sanskrit: "ātmasaṃsthaṃ śivaṃ tyaktvā..."</note>

71. He who, leaving the <term>Śiva</term> (God) who is inside, worships that which is outside (*viz.*, worships external forms), is like one who throws away the sweetmeat <note>The original term "pinda" refers to a ball of food or rice offered in rituals; here it symbolizes the spiritual nourishment already in one's possession.</note> in his hand, and wanders away in search of food.

->One should perform the worship of the self-existent Linga daily without laziness. All success will be his; of this there should be no doubt.<- <note>original Sanskrit: "ātmaliṅgārcanaṃ kuryād..."</note>

72. Let one thus meditate daily, without negligence, on his own <term>Swayambhu-linga</term>; and have no doubts that from this will come all powers.

->By constant practice, success is gained in six months. His breath will certainly enter the middle channel, the <term>Sushumna</term>.<- <note>original Sanskrit: "nirantarakṛtābhyāsa..."</note>

73. By habitual exercise, he gets success in six months; and undoubtedly his <term>vâyu</term> <note>Vital breath or internal energy (prana).</note> enters the middle channel (the <term>Sushumnâ</term> <note>The central spiritual nerve or channel in the subtle body through which energy rises.</note>).

->He gains mastery over the mind by restraining the breath and the vital seed. There is no doubt that he achieves success in both this world and the next.<- <note>original Sanskrit: "manojayañca labhate..."</note>

74. He conquers the mind, and can restrain his breath and his semen; then he gets success in this as well as the other world, without doubt.

### 2. <term>Swâdhisthân Chakra</term>. (<term>Prostatic Plexus</term>).

### 3. Manipur Chakra.

79. The third Chakra, called <term>Manipur</term><note>The "City of Jewels," located at the navel and associated with the element of fire and personal power.</note>, is situated near the navel; it is of a golden color and has ten petals marked with the letters *d, dh, n, t, th, d, dh, n, p,* and *ph*.

80. Its presiding adept is called Rudra—the giver of all auspicious things—and the presiding goddess of this place is the most sacred Lâkini.

81. The yogi who constantly practices meditation on this *Manipur* chakra obtains the <term>Pâtâla siddhi</term><note>The power to master the subterranean realms or hidden treasures of the earth.</note>, which brings continuous happiness. In this world, he gains whatever he desires, sees the destruction of sorrow and disease, learns to cheat death itself, and gains the power to enter the bodies of others.

### 6. The Command Center (Ajña Chakra)

> ->Now, the description of the Command Center (Ajña Chakra).<-
> ->The Command Lotus is located between the two eyebrows; it has two petals marked with the letters *ha* and *ksha*.<-
> ->It is white in color; there dwells the perfected "Great Time" (Mahâkâla), and the goddess here is Hâkinî. [96]<-
<note>The Ajña Chakra is often called the "Third Eye" and is considered the seat of intuition and command.</note>

> ->Within those petals, the seed-syllable <insert>हूँ (Hum)</insert> is displayed, brilliant as the autumnal moon.<-
> ->The wise seeker, knowing this "Supreme Swan," is never pulled down into worldly suffering. [97]<-
<note>The original OCR mentions the syllable "tham," but the Sanskrit insertion is "Hum." The "Supreme Swan" (Paramahamsa) refers to a soul that has achieved the highest state of spiritual discrimination.</note>

> ->This is the supreme light celebrated in all the <term>Tantras</term>.<-
> ->By contemplating this, one obtains the highest success; of this, there is no doubt. [98]<-
<term>Tantras: Ancient esoteric scriptures providing practical methods for spiritual expansion and ritual.</term>

<note>The text interprets the sacred city of Varanasi (Benares) as a location within the human body. The Ida and Pingala are the left and right energy channels; their meeting point in the head is equated to the holy city where the god Shiva (Vishwanâtha) resides.</note>

### 7. The Thousand-Petalled Lotus.

->**The central channel <term>Sushumnâ</term> goes along the spinal column to where the opening of the Creator <term>Brahmarandhra</term> is situated.**<-
->**Then, turning from there, it goes to the right of the Command <term>Âjñâ</term> lotus.**<-
->**It reaches the left nostril and is sung of as the "Ganges." (102)**<-

102. The <term>Sushumnâ</term> <note>The primary energy channel that runs through the center of the spine.</note> goes along the spinal cord up to where the <term>Brahmarandhra</term> <note>Literally the "Hole of Brahma," located at the crown of the head; it is considered the gateway to spiritual liberation.</note> (the hole of Brahma) is situated. Thence by a certain flexure, it goes to the right side of the <term>Âjñâ</term> <note>The sixth chakra, or "Command" center, located between the eyebrows.</note> lotus, whence it proceeds to the left nostril, and is called the Ganges.

->**In the opening of the Creator, the lotus called the Thousand-Petalled <term>Sahasrâra</term> is established.**<-
->**In its center is a source where the Moon is situated.**<-
->**From its triangular shape, nectar <note>original: "sudha"</note> flows continually.**<-
->**The Moon pours nectar into the lunar channel <term>Idâ</term> in a balanced way.**<-
->**It carries the nectar in a constant stream—a continuous flow.**<-
->**It reaches the left nostril and is indeed called the "Ganges" by the Yogis. (103)**<-

103. The lotus which is situated in the <term>Brahmarandhra</term> is called <term>Sahasrâra</term> <note>The "Thousand-petalled" crown chakra, representing the highest state of consciousness.</note> (the thousand-petalled). In the space in its centre, dwells the moon. From that triangular place, elixir <note>The nectar of immortality, or "Amrita," believed to be generated during deep meditation.</note> is continually exuding. This moon-fluid of immortality unceasingly flows through the <term>Idâ</term> <note>The "Lunar" energy channel on the left side of the body.</note>. The elixir flows in a stream,—a continuous stream. Going to the left nostril, it receives from the Yogis the name of the "Ganges."

->**From the right side of the Command <term>Âjñâ</term> lotus, going to the left nostril,**<-
->**flowing northward, the lunar channel <term>Idâ</term> is there called the "Varana." (104)**<-

104. From the right-side portion of the <term>Âjñâ</term> lotus and going to the left nostril flows the <term>Idâ</term>. It is here called <term>Varana</term> <note>One of the two rivers that give the sacred city of Varanasi its name.</note> (the northward-flowing Ganges).

->**Between those two, one should contemplate the city of "Varanasi."**<-
->**In the same way, the solar channel <term>Pingalâ</term> exists within that Command <term>Âjñâ</term> lotus.**<-
->**It goes to the right nostril and has been called the "Asi." (105)**<-

105. Let the Yogi contemplate on the space between the two (<term>Idâ</term> and <term>Pingalâ</term> <note>The "Solar" energy channel on the right side of the body.</note>) as <term>Vârânasi</term> <note>Also known as Benares, the holiest city in India; the text suggests the city exists spiritually within the practitioner's own head.</note> (Benares). The <term>Pingalâ</term> also comes in the same way from the left side portion of the <term>Âjñâ</term> lotus, and goes to the right nostril, and has been called by us the <term>Asi</term> <note>The second river of the city of Varanasi.</note>.

# CHAPTER V. 106-112.

-> In the Root Support <term>Mūlādhāra</term>, there is a lotus fixed with four petals. Within its center is the source <note>original Sanskrit: "yoni"</note>, and within that, the sun is situated. <-

108. The venom (the sun-fluid of mortality) which flows there continuously in a stream goes to the right nostril, just as the moon-fluid of immortality goes to the left. <note>In this tradition, the right side is associated with the sun/heat/consumption, while the left is associated with the moon/coolness/preservation.</note>

Above that, three more sacred seats are described by those who contemplate Yoga. They are known as the Point <term>Bindu</term>, the Sound <term>Nāda</term>, and the Power <term>Śakti</term>, situated in the lotus of the forehead. <-

110. The two-petalled Command Center <term>Ājñā</term>-lotus has been thus described where dwells the God **Maheshwara** <note>A name for Shiva, the Great Lord.</note>. The Yogis describe three more sacred stages above this. They are called the Point <term>Bindu</term>, the Sound <term>Nāda</term>, and the Power <term>Śakti</term>, and are situated in the lotus of the forehead.

-> He who always performs secret meditation on the Command Center <term>Ājñā</term> lotus destroys the actions <term>karmas</term> performed in previous lives without any resistance. <-

111. He who always contemplates on the hidden Command Center <term>Ājñā</term> lotus, at once destroys all the *karmas* <note>The spiritual law of cause and effect resulting from past deeds.</note> of his past life, without any opposition. <note>The word "opposition" is misspelled in the original source.</note>

-> While staying in this place, when the Yogi meditates constantly, then icons, worship, and prayers appear to him as worthless. <-

112. Remaining in this place, when the Yogi meditates constantly, then to him all forms, worships and prayers appear as worthless. <note>This suggests that internal realization supersedes the need for external religious rituals.</note>

-> Nature spirits <term>Yakṣas</term>, demons <term>Rākṣasas</term>, celestial musicians <term>Gandharvas</term>, nymphs <term>Apsaras</term>, and mythical beings <term>Kinnaras</term> serve his feet; all are under his control. <-

# SIVA SAMHITA.

113. The <term>Yakshas</term> <note>Nature spirits associated with the earth and treasures.</note>, <term>Râkshashas</term> <note>Powerful celestial beings, sometimes depicted as fierce or demonic.</note>, <term>Gandharvas</term> <note>Celestial musicians and singers of the gods.</note>, <term>Apsarâs</term> <note>Celestial nymphs and dancers.</note>, and <term>Kinnaras</term> <note>Mythical beings known for their musical skill, often depicted as half-human and half-horse or bird.</note>, all serve at his feet. They become obedient to his command.

->The Yogi turns the tongue backward, entering it into the<-
->upper hollows of the palate, holding the meditation that destroys fear.<-
->In this place, whoever's mind remains steady for even half a second,<-
->all their sins go to complete destruction at that very moment. 114.<-

114. By reversing the tongue and placing it in the long hollow of the palate <note>original: "lambikordhveshu garteshu" — This refers to the practice of Khechari Mudra, where the tongue is curled back to touch the soft palate or enter the nasal cavity to stimulate spiritual centers.</note>, let the <term>Yogi</term> <note>A practitioner of yoga who seeks spiritual union.</note> enter into contemplation, that destroys all fears. All his sins, whose mind remains steady here even for a second,—are at once destroyed.

->Whatever rewards were mentioned for each of the<-
->five lotuses, all of those certainly<-
->arise simply from the knowledge of this one center. 115.<-

115. All the fruits which have been described above as resulting from the contemplation of the other five <term>lotuses</term> <note>The primary chakras or energy centers located along the spine: Muladhara, Svadhishthana, Manipura, Anahata, and Vishuddha.</note>, are obtained through the knowledge of this one <term>Âjña lotus</term> <note>The "Command" chakra, located between the eyebrows, often called the Third Eye.</note> alone.

->The wise one who constantly practices<-
->meditation on the Command Center,<-
->rejoices after casting off the great bond of desire. 116.<-

116. The wise one, who continually practises contemplation of this <term>Âjña lotus</term>, becomes free from the mighty chain of desires, and enjoys happiness.

->At the time the breath departs, the wise one who remembers<-
->this lotus and leaves the life-breath, that righteous soul<-
->merges into the Supreme Soul. 117.<-

117. When at the time of death, the Yogi contemplates on this lotus, leaving this life, that holy one is absorbed in the <term>Paramâtmâ</term> <note>The Supreme Soul or Universal Consciousness, the ultimate reality in Hindu philosophy.</note>.

->Whether standing, walking, sleeping, or waking, the man who<-
->meditates, even if he were to perform sinful acts,<-
->does not sink into the depths of sin. 118.<-

118. He who contemplates on this, standing or walking, sleeping or waking, is not touched by sins, even if it were possible for him to do sinful works.

->The Yogi is released from bondage by his own radiance.<-
->The majesty of the meditation on the two-petalled lotus cannot be fully told;<-
->Even the gods like the Creator and others know only a small portion of its grandeur from me. 119.<-

119. The <term>Yogi</term> becomes free from the chain by his own exertion. The importance of the contemplation of the <term>two-petalled lotus</term> <note>A reference to the Âjña Chakra, which is traditionally depicted with two white petals representing the two major psychic nerves, Ida and Pingala.</note> cannot be fully described. Even the gods like <term>Brahmâ</term> <note>The Hindu god of creation.</note>, etc., have learnt only a portion of its grandeur from me <note>The speaker here is Shiva, addressing his consort Parvati.</note>.

<summary>This page concludes the description of the Âjña Chakra, explaining that meditation on this single "two-petalled" center grants all the spiritual powers of the lower chakras and leads to liberation from desire and sin. It specifically highlights the practice of Khechari Mudra (curling the tongue) as a method to achieve a state of absorption into the Supreme Soul at the moment of death.</summary>
<keywords>Ajña Chakra, Khechari Mudra, Paramatma, spiritual liberation, two-petalled lotus, Shiva Samhita, meditation benefits</keywords>

### The Thousand-Petalled Lotus.

**120. Above this, at the base of the palate, is the <term>Sahasrara|The "Thousand-Petalled" lotus located at the crown of the head</term>. It is situated where the opening of the <term>Sushumna|The central energy channel of the subtle body</term> resides.** 
<note>original: "अत ऊर्ध्वं तालुमूले सहस्रारंसरोरुहम् । अस्ति यत्र सुषुम्णाया मूलं सविवरं स्थितम् ॥ १२० ॥"</note>

**121. From the base of the palate, that central channel extends downward until it reaches the <term>Muladhara|The "Root-Support" center at the base of the spine</term> and the perineum. All the energy vessels surround it or are supported by it. These <term>Nadis|Subtle channels or vessels through which life-force (prana) flows</term> are the seeds of the essential mystery and the source of all principles that make up a human being; they reveal the path to <term>Brahma|The Absolute or the Creator</term>, leading to ultimate salvation.** 
<note>original: "तालुमूले सुषुम्णा सा अधोवक्त्रा प्रवर्तते । मूलाधारेणोयन्त्यस्ताः सर्वनाड्यः समाश्रिताः ॥ ता बीजभूतास्तत्त्वस्य ब्रह्ममार्गप्रदायिकाः ॥ १२१ ॥"</note>

**122. The lotus located at the root of the palate is known as the <term>Sahasrara</term>. In its center, there is a <term>Yoni|A sacred seat, womb, or focal point of creative force</term> which is said to face downward.** 
<note>original: "तालुस्थाने च यत्पद्मं सहस्रारं पुराहितम् । तत्कन्दे योनिरैकास्ति पश्चिमाभिमुखी मता ॥ १२२ ॥"</note>

**123. Within that center lies the root of the central channel along with its opening. This is called the <term>Brahmarandhra|The "Opening of Brahma," the spiritual gateway at the top of the skull</term>, which extends all the way down to the root-support lotus.** 
<note>original: "तस्या मध्ये सुषुम्णाया मूलं सविवरं स्थितम् । ब्रह्मरन्ध्रं तदे्वोक्तमामूलाधारपङ्कजम् ॥ १२३ ॥"</note>

**124. Inside that opening of the central channel, the <term>Kundalini|The "Coiled One," the dormant spiritual energy often depicted as a serpent</term> always resides as its inner force. Within the central channel, my beloved, there is also a constant current of energy called the <term>Chitra|The "Bright" or "Variegated" inner layer of the central channel</term>. In my view, the meditations on the "Opening of Brahma" and the like should be performed within this channel.** 
<note>original: "ततस्तद्रन्ध्रे तच्छक्तिः सुषुम्णा कुण्डली सदा । सुषुम्णायां सदा शक्तिश्चित्रा स्यान्मम वल्लभे ॥ तस्यां मम मते कार्या ब्रह्मरन्ध्रादिकल्पना ॥ १२४ ॥"</note>

**125. By simply remembering this, one attains the knowledge of the Absolute. All sins are destroyed, and the person is never again reborn into the human condition.** 
<note>original: "यस्याः स्मरणमात्रेण ब्रह्मज्ञत्वं प्रजायते । पापक्षयश्च भवति न भूयः पुरुषो भवेत् ॥ १२५ ॥"</note>

**126. Having forcefully introduced [the breath], one should hold it within their own mouth. By this practice, the vital airs that move through the body cease to flow [outwardly].** 
<note>original: "प्रवेशितं चलादृष्टं मुखे स्वस्य निवेशयेत् । तेनात्र न वहत्येव देहचारी समीरणाः ॥ १२६ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page describes the Sahasrara (Thousand-Petalled Lotus) at the crown of the head and its connection via the Sushumna channel to the Muladhara at the base of the spine. It identifies these internal structures as the source of spiritual liberation and the residence of the Kundalini energy.</summary>
<keywords>Sahasrara, Sushumna, Muladhara, Nadis, Brahmarandhra, Kundalini, Chitra, liberation, subtle body anatomy, yoga philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 80 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for breath control and internal energy work within the Siva Samhita. It specifically details the movement of vital energy from the base of the spine to the crown of the head.</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA.

126. Let the practitioner thrust the moving thumb into its mouth: by this action, the air that flows through the body is brought to a standstill.

->By this breath, one wanders eternally within the wheel of worldly existence.<-
->Therefore, the Yogi strives not for the mere retention of breath,<-
->But to overcome the eight-fold knots that obstruct the channels.<-
->For only the Kundalini power can leave the opening; there is no other way. ॥ 127 ॥<-

127. Because of this movement of <term>vâyu: the vital life-force or breath</term>, a person wanders through the endless cycle of the universe. Therefore, the Yogis do not desire to simply maintain this circulation of breath; all the <term>nâdis: energy channels</term> are bound by eight knots. Only the <term>kundalini: the primordial energy coiled at the base of the spine</term> can pierce through these knots, pass out of the <term>Brahmarandhra: the "Opening of Brahman" located at the crown of the head</term>, and reveal the way to ultimate salvation.

->When the vessels are fully filled and the internal winds are confined,<-
->Then, by the release of these bonds, the face of the Kundalini emerges from the opening. ॥ 128 ॥<-

128. When the air is fully confined within all the vessels of the body, the Kundalini releases itself from these spiritual knots and forces its way out through the crown opening.

->The vital breath should always flow through the central channel.<-
->At the root lotus, the source is situated between the left and right corners.<-
->Between the Ida and the Pingala, the Sushumna resides in the center of that source. ॥ 129 ॥<-

129. Then the vital air flows continually through the <term>Sushumnâ: the central spiritual channel of the spine</term>. On the right and left sides of the <term>Mulâdhâr: the root chakra or base center</term> are situated the <term>Idâ: the lunar or left-side channel</term> and the <term>Pingalâ: the solar or right-side channel</term>. The Sushumnâ passes directly through the middle of them.

->The crown opening is located right there, in the sphere of the Sushumna's base.<-
->The wise one who understands this is liberated from the bonds of karma. ॥ 130 ॥<-

130. The hollow space of the Sushumnâ within the sphere of the root center is called the Brahmarandhra. The wise practitioner who recognizes this is emancipated from the chains of <term>karma: the cycle of action and consequence</term>.

->At the mouth of the crown opening, these three channels undoubtedly meet.<-
->By bathing at this junction, the practitioner attains liberation without obstacle. ॥ 131 ॥<-

131. All these three vessels—the Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna—certainly meet at the entrance of the crown opening; by "bathing" <note>In this context, "bathing" refers to the internal meditative union of energies rather than a physical washing.</note> at this sacred internal place, one certainly obtains salvation.

->*The Sacred Triveni (The Confluence at Prayâg).*<-

->Between the Ganges and the Jumna flows this Saraswati.<-
->By bathing at their meeting point, the fortunate one reaches the highest state. ॥ 132 ॥<-

132. Between the <note>original: "Ganga"</note> Ganges and the <note>original: "Jamuna"</note> Jumna flows the invisible <note>original: "Saraswati"</note> Saraswati. By bathing at their junction <note>The text uses the geography of India's most sacred rivers as a metaphor for the intersection of the three primary energy channels in the human body.</note>, the fortunate practitioner attains the highest state of spiritual evolution.

<summary>This page describes how the Kundalini energy pierces the eight knots of the energetic body to reach the Brahmarandhra (crown). It uses the allegory of the Triveni (the confluence of the Ganges, Jumna, and Saraswati rivers) to explain the meeting of the three primary nâdis.</summary>
<keywords>Siva Samhita, Kundalini, Brahmarandhra, Sushumna, Ida, Pingala, Triveni, Prayag, Karma, Vayu, Muladhar, Nadi, spiritual liberation, breath control</keywords>

# CHAPTER V. 133-139. 75

---

->It was said of old that the Ida is the Ganges and the Pingala is the Daughter of the Sun.<-
->The middle one is called the Saraswati; the meeting of these is very difficult to attain. || 133 ||<-
<note>original: "इडा गंगा पुरा प्रोक्ता पिंगला चार्कपुत्रिका । मध्या सरस्वती प्रोक्ता तासां संगोऽतिदुर्लभः ॥ १३३ ॥"</note>

133. We have said before that the *Iḍâ* is the <term>Ganges</term> <note>A primary holy river of India; here it represents the left energy channel associated with lunar, cooling energy</note> and the *Pingalâ* is the daughter of the sun (the <term>Jamuna</term> <note>The Yamuna river; here representing the right energy channel associated with solar, heating energy</note>), in the middle the *Sushumnâ* is the <term>Saraswati</term> <note>A mystical, invisible river; here representing the central channel of the spine through which spiritual energy rises</note>;—the place where all three join is a most inaccessible one.

->At the confluence of the White and the Black, he who performs a bath with his mind,<-
->being freed from all sins, goes to the eternal Brahma. || 134 ||<-
<note>original: "सितासिते संगमे यो मनसा स्नानमाचरेत् । सर्वपापविनिर्मुक्तो याति ब्रह्म सनातनम् ॥ ३४ ॥"</note>

134. He who performs mental bathing at the junction of the White (*Iḍâ*) and the Black (*Pingalâ*) becomes free from all sins, and reaches the eternal <term>Brahma</term> <note>The ultimate reality or absolute ground of being in Hindu philosophy</note>.

->At the confluence of the three rivers, he who performs the rites for the ancestors,<-
->having saved all his ancestors, goes to the highest goal. || 135 ||<-
<note>original: "त्रिवेण्यां संगमे यो वै पितृकर्म समाचरेत् । तारयित्वा पितृन्सर्वांन्स याति परमां गतिम् ॥ १३५ ॥"</note>

135. He who performs the funeral rites of his ancestors at the junction of these three rivers (<term>Triveni</term> <note>Literally "three braids," referring to the sacred meeting point of the Ganges, Yamuna, and Saraswati rivers</note>) procures salvation for his ancestors and himself reaches the highest end.

->The daily, the occasional, and the optional duties—he who performs these every day,<-
->by meditating with the mind, obtains an unfading reward. || 136 ||<-
<note>original: "नित्यं नैमित्तिकं काम्यं प्रत्यहं यः समाचरेत् । मनसा चिन्तयित्वा तु सोऽक्षयं फलमाप्नुयात् ॥ १३६ ॥"</note>

136. He who daily performs the threefold duties (*i.e.*, the regular, occasional and the optional ones <note>In Vedic tradition, these are Nitya (daily obligations), Naimittika (special occasions), and Kamya (rituals performed for a specific desired result)</note>) by mentally meditating on this place, receives the unfading reward.

->He who performs a bath but once enjoys the happiness of heaven;<-
->his manifold sins truly burned, the Yogi himself becomes pure of mind. || 137 ||<-
<note>original: "सकृद्यः कुरुते स्नानं स्वर्गे सौख्यं भुनक्ति सः । दग्ध्वा पापानशेषान् वै योगी शुद्धमतिः स्वयम् ॥ १३७ ॥"</note>

137. He who once bathes at this sacred place enjoys heavenly felicity, his manifold sins are burned, he becomes a pure-minded <term>Yogi</term> <note>A practitioner of yoga who seeks spiritual union and self-mastery</note>.

->Whether impure or pure, or in whatever state one might be,<-
->simply by performing the act of bathing, one becomes holy; there is no other way. || 138 ||<-
<note>original: "अपवित्रः पवित्रो वा सर्वावस्थां गतोऽपि वा । स्नानाचरणमात्रेण पूतो भवति नान्यथा ॥ १३८ ॥"</note>

138. Whether pure or impure, in whatever state one might be, by performing ablution at this mystic place, he becomes undoubtedly holy.

->At the time of death, when the body is immersed in the water of the Triveni,<-
->thinking of which he gives up his life, he then reaches liberation. || 139 ||<-
<note>original: "मृत्युकाले प्लुतं देहं त्रिवेण्याः सलिले यदा । विचिन्त्य यस्त्यजेत्प्राणन्स तदा मोक्षमाप्नुयात् ॥ १३९ ॥"</note>

139. At the time of death let him bathe himself in the water of this *Triveni* (the Trinity of rivers): he who dies thinking on this, reaches salvation then and there.

->There is no secret more supreme than this in the three worlds.<-
->It must be kept hidden with great effort and should never be explained. || 140 ||<-
<note>original: "नातःपरतरं गुह्यं त्रिषु लोकेषु विद्यते । गोप्यं तत्प्रयत्नेन न व्याख्येयं कदाचन ॥ १४० ॥"</note>

<summary>This page describes the internal "Triveni" or confluence of the three primary energy channels (Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna) as a mystical equivalent to India's most sacred rivers. It asserts that mental meditation on this junction provides the same spiritual purification and merit as physical pilgrimage and traditional rites.</summary>

<keywords>Ida, Pingala, Sushumna, Ganges, Jamuna, Saraswati, Brahma, Triveni, Yogi, Moksha, funeral rites, salvation, subtle body, nadis, meditation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discourse on the internal mystical anatomy and the specific meditation techniques related to the crown of the head, focusing on the Brahmarandhra and the Sahasrâra lotus.</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA.

140. There is no greater secret than this throughout the three worlds. This should be kept secret with great care. It ought never to be revealed.

141. If the mind becomes steadily fixed even for half a second at the <term>Brahmarandhra</term> <note>The "Aperture of Brahman," a spiritual gateway at the crown of the head</note>, one becomes free from all sins and reaches the highest spiritual state.

142. The holy <term>Yogi</term> whose mind is absorbed in this is absorbed in me <note>Shiva, the speaker of this text, representing the supreme consciousness</note>, after having enjoyed the supernatural powers called <term>animâ</term> <note>The ability to become as small as an atom</note>, <term>laghimâ</term> <note>The ability to become weightless</note>, and others.

143. The person knowing this <term>Brahmarandhra</term> becomes my beloved in this world; by conquering sins, they become eligible for liberation; by sharing this knowledge, they help save thousands of people.

144. The <term>Four-faced</term> <note>original: "Chaturmukha," referring to Brahma the Creator</note> and the other gods can hardly obtain this knowledge. It is the most invaluable treasure of the <term>Yogis</term>; this mystery of the <term>Brahmarandhra</term> should be kept a great secret.

->*The Moon of Mystery.*<-

145. I have said before that there is a force-centre or <term>yoni</term> <note>A sacred place of origin or power</note> in the middle of the <term>Sahasrâra</term> <note>The "thousand-petaled" lotus at the top of the head</note>; below that is the moon; let the wise contemplate this.

146. By remembering this, the great <term>Yogi</term> becomes worthy of worship in this world and is respected by the gods and the <term>siddhas</term> <note>Perfected masters or enlightened beings</note>.

147. Within the hollow of the skull, let the practitioner contemplate the <term>ocean of milk</term> <note>A symbolic representation of pure, cooling divine consciousness</note>; from that place within the <term>Sahasrâra</term> lotus, let them meditate upon the moon.

<summary>This page describes the spiritual importance of the Brahmarandhra (the crown of the head) and the Sahasrâra lotus, explaining that meditation on these points leads to liberation, supernatural powers, and divine favor.</summary>
<keywords>Brahmarandhra, Sahasrâra, Shiva, meditation, liberation, animâ, laghimâ, moon meditation, ocean of milk, yogic secrets</keywords>

# CHAPTER V. 148-151.

->Within the opening of the skull, let him contemplate that stainless one, known as the nectar-rayed Moon-Swan.<- <note>original: "shira-kapala-vivare... piyusha-bhanu-hamsa-akhyam"</note>
->By constant practice, he surely sees it within three days.<-
->By the mere sight of it, the practitioner burns away the mountain of his sins.<-

148. In the *sinus* <note>the hollow cavity or space</note> of the forehead there is the nectar-containing moon, having sixteen digits <term>kalas; the sixteen phases of the moon representing different aspects of energy</term>. Let him contemplate on this stainless one. By constant practice, he sees it in three days. By merely seeing it, the practitioner burns all his sins.

->The future reveals itself and the mind becomes truly pure.<-
->Even if he has committed the five great sins, he burns them away by doing this for even a moment.<-

149. The future reveals itself to him, his mind becomes pure; and though he might have committed the <term>five great sins; traditionally these include the most serious transgressions such as killing a priest or scholar, theft, and drinking intoxicants</term>, by a moment's contemplation of this he destroys them.

->The planets become favorable and all obstacles perish.<-
->Disturbances are calmed and he achieves victory in battle.<-
->The powers of moving through the air and moving upon the earth are achieved by seeing this Moon of Milk.<-
->Everything happens through meditation alone; one should not doubt this.<-
->Through constant practice of Yoga, one becomes a perfected adept, not otherwise.<-
->Truly, truly, and again truly, he shall certainly become my equal.<-
->Devotion to the science of Yoga gives success to the Yogis.<-

150. All the heavenly bodies <note>the planets and stars which influence human destiny</note> become auspicious, all dangers are destroyed, all accidents are warded off, success is obtained in war; the <term>Khechari; the power to move through the sky or higher dimensions of consciousness</term> and the <term>Bhuchari; the power to move at will across the earthly plane</term> powers are acquired by the seeing of the moon which is in the head. By mere contemplation on it all these results ensue, there is no doubt of it. By constant practice of Yoga one verily becomes an adept. Verily, verily, again most verily, he becomes certainly my equal <note>The narrator is the god Shiva, suggesting the practitioner achieves a state of god-like consciousness</note>. The continual study of the science of Yoga, gives success to the Yogis.

->Here ends the description of the <term>Ajñapura Chakra; the 'Command Center' or Third Eye located between the eyebrows</term>.<-
->The Mystic Mount Kailâs <note>Symbolically located at the crown of the head, representing the home of pure consciousness</note>.<-

### **The Discourse on Raja Yoga** <note>original: "Atha Rajayoga-kathanām"</note>

->Above this is the divine, beautiful Thousand-Petalled Lotus.<-
->It stands outside the body known as the Microcosm, and it bestows liberation.<-

151. Above this <note>above the lunar sphere previously described</note> is the brilliant thousand-petalled lotus <term>Sahasrara</term>. It is outside this <term>microcosm; the "Egg of Brahma" or the human body seen as a miniature version of the universe</term> of the body, it is the giver of salvation.

<summary>This page concludes the instructions for meditating on the lunar center within the forehead, detailing the spiritual and psychic powers gained from the practice. It introduces the Sahasrara, the crown chakra, as a transcendent center located beyond the physical body that leads to final liberation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the description of the subtle body's internal geography, specifically focusing on the highest spiritual center located at the crown of the head, identified here as the mystical Mount Kailas.</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA
**Page 78**

->**That place is indeed called Kailas, where the Great Lord resides. He is known as Nakula, the eternal one, who is free from both growth and decay. [152]**<-
<note>original: "कैलासो नाम तस्यैव महेशो यत्र तिष्ठति । नकुलाख्योऽविनाशी च क्षयवृद्धिविवर्जितः ॥"</note>
<term>Kailas</term> <note>While Kailas is a physical mountain in the Himalayas, in this context it refers to the Sahasrara or the "thousand-petaled lotus" at the crown of the head.</note>
<term>Nakula</term> <note>A name for Shiva meaning "not of the family" or "beyond lineage," signifying his absolute, unconditioned nature.</note>

152. Its name is truly the *<term>Kailas</term>* mount, where dwells the great Lord Shiva, who is called <term>Nakula</term> and exists without destruction, and without increase or decrease.

->**By simply knowing this place, humans are never again born into this world of suffering. Through constant practice of this union, they gain the full power to create or destroy all living beings. [153]**<-
<note>original: "स्थानस्यास्य ज्ञानमात्रेण नृणां, संसारेऽस्मिन्सम्भवो नैव भूयः । भूतग्रामं सन्तताभ्यासयोगात्कर्तुं हर्तुं स्याच्च शक्तिः समग्रा ॥"</note>

153. Men, as soon as they discover this most secret place, become free from re-births in this universe. By the practice of this <term>Yoga</term> <note>Yoga: here meaning the disciplined practice of meditation and breath control to achieve spiritual union.</note> he gets the power of creating or destroying the creation, this aggregate of elements.

->**In this supreme place, the dwelling of the "Great Swan," known as Kailas, if one fixes their mind, the practitioner is cured of disease, overcomes all suffering, and lives for a vast age, freed from the cycle of death. [154]**<-
<note>original: "स्थाने परे हंसनिवासभूते, कैलासनाम्नीह निविष्टचेताः । योगी हृतव्याधिरधः कृताधिवरायुश्चिरं जीवति मृत्युमुक्तः ॥"</note>
<term>Great Swan</term> <note>Sanskrit: Hamsa. A symbol of the soul or the vital breath (Prana) that moves between the material and spiritual worlds.</note>

154. When the mind is steadily fixed at this place, which is the residence of the Great Swan and is called *<term>Kailas</term>*, then that <term>Yogi</term> <note>Yogi: a practitioner of Yoga.</note>, devoid of diseases and subduing all accidents, lives for a great age, free from death.

->**When the movements of the mind are dissolved in the Great Lord called the source, the practitioner attains the stillness of perfect meditative absorption. [155]**<-
<note>original: "चित्तवृत्तिर्यदा लीना कुलाख्ये परमेश्वरे । तदा समाधिसाम्येन योगी निश्चलतां व्रजेत् ॥"</note>

155. When the mind of the <term>Yogi</term> is absorbed in the Great God called the *<term>Kula</term>* <note>Kula: literally "family" or "source," referring here to the ultimate reality or the Shakti energy returning to its source.</note>, then the fullness of the *<term>Samadhi</term>* <note>Samadhi: a state of intense concentration where the individual consciousness merges with the universal.</note> is attained, and the <term>Yogi</term> achieves steadfastness.

->**Through constant meditation, the world is forgotten; then, undoubtedly, the practitioner gains extraordinary powers. [156]**<-
<note>original: "निरन्तरकृते ध्याने जगद्विस्मरणं भवेत् । तदा विचित्रसामर्थ्यं योगिनो भवति ध्रुवम् ॥"</note>

156. By constant meditation one forgets the world, then in truth the <term>Yogi</term> obtains wonderful power.

->**Therefore, let the practitioner drink the nectar that flows from there. By doing so, they conquer death and master the source of power. Here, the coiled energy called Kundalini dissolves, and the fourfold creation is absorbed into the Supreme Soul. [157]**<-
<note>original: "तस्माद्गलितपीयूषं पिबेद्योगी निरन्तरम् । मृत्योर्मृत्युं विधायाशु कुलं जित्वा सरोरुहे । अत्र कुण्डलिनी शक्तिर्लयं याति कुलाभिधा । तदा चतुर्विधा सृष्टिल्लीयते परमात्मनि ॥"</note>

157. Let the <term>Yogi</term> continually drink the nectar <note>The "nectar" (Amrita) is described in yogic texts as a subtle fluid that drips from the crown center during deep meditation, granting longevity.</note> which flows out of it; by this he gives law to death, and conquers the *<term>Kula</term>*. Here the *<term>Kula</term>* *<term>Kundalini</term>* <note>Kundalini: the fundamental life force, often depicted as a coiled serpent at the base of the spine, which rises through the centers to the crown.</note> force is absorbed; after this, the quadruple creation is absorbed in the <term>Param Atman</term> <note>Param Atman: The Supreme Soul or Absolute Reality.</note>.

### The Royal Path of Union
<note>original: "अथ राजयोगः" (The Raja Yoga)</note>

->**The practitioner, seeking no reward, works toward that state in which the mind dissolves upon reaching its goal. [158]**<-
<note>original: "यज्ज्ञात्वा प्राप्य विषयं चित्तवृत्तिर्विलीयते । तस्मिन् परिश्रमं योगी करोति निरपेक्षः ॥"</note>

158. Having known this and having reached the object of meditation, the activities of the mind dissolve; the <term>Yogi</term> works toward this state without expectation of reward.

<summary>This page describes the spiritual benefits of meditating on the crown center (Kailas), including the attainment of immortality, supernatural powers, and the absorption of the individual soul into the Supreme Reality. It concludes by introducing Raja Yoga, the "Royal Path" of mental mastery.</summary>

<keywords>Kailas, Shiva, Nakula, Yoga, Samadhi, Kula, Kundalini, Param Atman, Raja Yoga, crown chakra, immortality, nectar, meditation</keywords>

### CHAPTER V. 158-166.

158. By this knowledge, the modifications of the mind <note>The Sanskrit term 'chitta-vritti' refers to the constant ripples or thoughts that disturb the mind's clarity.</note> are suspended, however active they may be: therefore, let the Yogi untiringly and unselfishly try to obtain this knowledge.

->The fluctuations of the mind are dissolved in that state, and the practitioner certainly becomes a Yogi; then is known the Indivisible, the form of pure knowledge, the stainless one. 159.<-
<note>original: "Cittavṛttirdalīnā tasmin yogī bhaved dhruvam | Tadā vijñāyate’khaṇḍज्ञानरूपो nirañjanaḥ"</note>

159. When the modifications of the thinking principle are suspended, then one certainly becomes a Yogi; then is known the Indivisible, holy, pure Gnosis <note>The translator uses 'Gnosis' here to mean spiritual knowledge or direct perception of reality.</note>.

->Having contemplated his own reflection outside the Cosmic Egg as previously described, let him enter into that and think of the Great Void without interruption. 160.<-
<note>original: "Brahmāṇḍabāhye saṃcitya svapratīkaṃ yathoditam..."</note>

160. Let him contemplate on his own reflection in the sky as beyond the <term>Cosmic Egg</term> <note>Sanskrit: 'Brahmanda'; the manifest universe or the sphere of creation.</note>: in the manner previously described. Through that let him think on the Great Void unceasingly.

->That Void is empty at the beginning, empty in the middle, and empty at the end; it has the brilliance of ten million suns, and the cool light of ten million moons. By practicing this, one obtains perfection. 161.<-

161. The Great Void, whose beginning is void, whose middle is void, whose end is void, has the brilliancy of tens of millions of suns, and the coolness of tens of millions of moons. By contemplating continually on this, one obtains success.

->Let him always perform this meditation daily without laziness; within a year, he shall attain all success—of this there is no doubt. 162.<-

162. Let him practise with energy daily this <term>dhyâna</term> <note>Meditation or sustained concentration on a single point or concept.</note>, within a year he will obtain all success undoubtedly.

->He whose mind remains steady there even for half a second is truly a Yogi and a true devotee, honored in all worlds. 163.<-

163. He whose mind is absorbed in that place even for a second, is certainly a Yogi, and a good devotee, and is reverenced in all worlds.

->The mass of his sins is destroyed at that very moment. 164.<-

164. All his stores of sins are at once verily destroyed.

->Seeing which, one does not return to the path of the world of death; let him practice this with effort by the path of the Swâdhisthân. 165.<-

165. By seeing it one never returns to the path of this mortal universe; let the Yogi, therefore, practise this with great care by the path of the <term>Swâdhisthân</term> <note>The second energy center or chakra, located in the pelvic region, associated with the 'self-established' seat.</note>.

->The greatness of this meditation cannot be described by me; he who achieves it knows it, and is esteemed by us. 166.<-

<summary>This page describes a specific meditation on the 'Great Void' located beyond the manifest universe, promising that its practice can destroy all sins and liberate the practitioner from the cycle of rebirth within a year.</summary>
<keywords>Great Void, dhyana, Shiva Samhita, Swadhisthan, Cosmic Egg, liberation from rebirth, mental modifications, yoga success</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discourse of Lord Shiva in the Siva Samhita, specifically moving from the benefits of contemplating the "void" (Sunya) to the introduction of the supreme "Rajadhiraj Yoga."</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA

<page-num>80</page-num>

166. I cannot describe the grandeur of this contemplation. He who practices it, knows its value. Such a practitioner becomes respected by me.

->Through meditation alone, one realizes the wonders that arise from such vision.<-
<note>original: "ध्यानादेव विजानाति विचित्रैक्षणसम्भवम्"</note>
->He undoubtedly becomes endowed with the qualities of smallness and lightness.<-
<note>original: "अणिमादिगुणोपेता भवत्येव न संशयः"</note>

167. By meditation, one at once knows the wonderful effects of this Yoga (that is, of the contemplation of the void); undoubtedly he attains the psychic powers, called <term>animâ</term> <note>the ability to become as small as an atom</note> and <term>laghimâ</term> <note>the ability to become weightless</note>, and others.

->I have declared the Royal Yoga, which is kept secret in all the Tantras.<-
<note>original: "राजयोगो मयाख्यातः सर्वतन्त्रेषु गोपितः"</note>
->Now I shall describe to you briefly this Yoga of the King of Kings.<-
<note>original: "राजाधिराजयोगोऽयं कथयामि समासतः"</note>

168. Thus have I described the <term>Râja Yoga</term> <note>The "Royal Yoga," a path focused on mastering the mind</note>; it is kept secret in all the <term>Tantras</term> <note>sacred esoteric texts</note>; now I shall describe to you briefly the <term>Râjâdhirâj Yoga</term> <note>The "Yoga of the King of Kings," considered the highest spiritual path</note>.

### *The Râjâdhirâj Yoga.*

->Having assumed the Auspicious Pose in a beautiful retreat free from animals,<-
<note>original: "स्वस्तिकासनं कृत्वा सुमठे जन्तुवर्जिते"</note>
->and having diligently honored the Guru, let him practice this meditation.<-
<note>original: "गुरुं संपूज्य यत्नेन ध्यानमेतत्ससाचरेत्"</note>

169. Sitting in the <term>Swastikâsana</term> <note>the "Auspicious Pose," a stable seated posture for meditation</note>, in a beautiful monastery or hermitage, free from the distractions of people and animals, and having paid respects to his <term>Guru</term> <note>spiritual teacher</note>, let the Yogi practice this contemplation.

->Knowing through the logic of Vedanta that the soul is independent,<-
<note>original: "निरालम्बं भवेज्जीवं ज्ञात्वा वेदान्तयुक्तिः"</note>
->and making the mind supportless, the wise one should think of nothing.<-
<note>original: "निरालम्बं मनः कृत्वा न किञ्चिच्चिन्तयेत्सुधीः"</note>

170. Knowing through the arguments of the <term>Vedanta</term> <note>the philosophical school based on the end of the Vedas, focusing on ultimate reality</note> that the <term>Jîva</term> <note>the individual soul or life-principle</note> is independent and self-supported, let him make his mind also self-supported; and let him not contemplate anything else.

->From this meditation, the Great Success certainly arises, without a doubt.<-
<note>original: "एतद्ध्यानान्महासिद्धिर्भवत्येव न संशयः"</note>
->By making the mind free of all modifications, one becomes Fullness itself.<-
<note>original: "वृत्तिहीनं मनः कृत्वा पूर्णरूपं स्वयं भवेत्"</note>

171. Undoubtedly, by this contemplation the highest success (<term>mahâ-siddhi</term> <note>supreme spiritual accomplishment</note>) is obtained by making the mind functionless; the seeker himself becomes perfectly "Full" or complete.

->The one who always practices this is truly a Yogi free from desire.<-
<note>original: "साधयेत्सततं यो वै स योगी विगतस्पृहः"</note>
->The name "I" no longer exists; only the Self is ever-present.<-
<note>original: "अहंनाम न कोप्यस्ति सर्वदात्मैव विद्यते"</note>

172. He who practices this always is the real passionless Yogi; he never uses the word “I,” but always finds himself filled with the <term>âtman</term> <note>the true, eternal Self</note>.

->What is bondage, and for whom is liberation? He always sees the One.<-
<note>original: "को बन्धः कस्य वा मोक्ष एकं पश्येत्सदा हि सः"</note>
->He who does this daily is surely liberated; of this there is no doubt.<-
<note>original: "एतत्करोति यो नित्यं स मुक्तो नात्र संशयः"</note>

173. What is bondage, and what is emancipation? To him, everything is always one; undoubtedly, he who practices this daily is truly emancipated.

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on the benefits of meditation on the void and introduces the Râjâdhirâj Yoga, emphasizing a state of mental stillness where the ego dissolves into the universal Self.</summary>

### CHAPTER V. 174-179.

->That very Yogi is a true devotee, honored in all worlds,<-
->who, recognizing the relationship of "I am" between the individual soul and the Supreme Soul,<-
->renounces the duality of "I" and "you" and meditates on the Indivisible Whole.<-
->In that state where everything is dissolved through the process of super-imposition and negation,<-
->the Yogi, free from all attachment, takes refuge in that Primal Seed.<-

174. He is the true Yogi and the true devotee, honored in every world, who contemplates the <term>Jivatma</term> <note>The individual soul or self as it exists in a physical body.</note> and the <term>Paramatma</term> <note>The Supreme Soul or Universal Spirit.</note> as being united in the realization of "I Am." He lets go of the distinction between "myself" and "the other" to focus on the indivisible reality. This Yogi, detached from the world, finds shelter in that contemplation where all things dissolve through the dual process of <term>super-imposition</term> <note>original: "Adhyaropa"; the mistake of attributing worldly qualities to the Divine, like seeing a "snake" when there is actually only a "rope."</note> and <term>negation</term> <note>original: "Apavada"; the corrective process of removing those false attributions to see the truth.</note>.

->Abandoning the directly perceived Bliss-Consciousness, the deluded ones—<-
->confused by their own errors—wander about arguing over what is hidden or manifest.<-

175. Turning away from the <term>Brahman</term> <note>The ultimate, absolute reality in Hindu philosophy.</note>—who is present right before them as pure knowledge and bliss—the confused wander aimlessly. They waste their efforts debating what is perceptible to the senses and what is beyond them.

->Whoever turns this universe of moving and unmoving things into an object of meditation,<-
->but abandons the directly manifest Supreme Brahman, is lost within this universe.<-

176. Anyone who meditates on this world of moving and still objects as if it were the ultimate reality, while ignoring the Supreme <term>Brahman</term> that is plainly manifest within, becomes absorbed and trapped by the material world.

->To ensure that the ignorance which blocks knowledge does not rise up again,<-
->the Yogi constantly practices while remaining entirely free from attachments.<-

177. To prevent the resurgence of the "Ignorance" that obscures spiritual insight, the Yogi—free from all worldly ties—dedicatedly maintains the practice that leads to <term>Gnosis</term> <note>original: "Jnana"; direct, experiential spiritual knowledge or wisdom.</note>.

->The wise one, restraining all the senses from their objects,<-
->remains free from all company, existing among objects as if in deep sleep.<-

178. A person of wisdom pulls their senses away from external attractions. By remaining detached from society, they live among the things of this world as if they were in a state of <term>deep sleep</term> <note>original: "Sushupti"; a state where the mind is still and does not perceive the external world, even while physically present.</note>—unaffected by what they see or hear.

->By practicing in this way daily, the Self-Luminous Light shines forth;<-
->the words of the Guru, meant to guide the student’s intellect, reach their end;<-
->and through the power of that practice, the One True Knowledge arises spontaneously.<-

179. Through this constant practice, the inner "Self-luminous" light reveals itself. At this stage, the verbal instructions of the <term>Guru</term> <note>A spiritual teacher or guide.</note> have fulfilled their purpose and are no longer needed. Through the sheer force of dedicated practice, the knowledge of the One arises naturally from within.

<summary>This page describes the pinnacle of Yogic practice where the practitioner realizes the unity of the individual and universal soul. It emphasizes moving past intellectual debate into a state of detached, direct experience of the Divine Light.</summary>
<keywords>Jivatma, Paramatma, Brahman, meditation, detachment, super-imposition, negation, Gnosis, Guru, self-luminosity</keywords>
