<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the practitioner (Yogi) as they transition from foundational practices to the realization of higher knowledge. It emphasizes the symbiotic relationship between physical and mental disciplines.</meta>

# SIVA SAMHITA.

From this point forward, the seeker must rely on their own efforts; others can no longer increase their reason or power. Henceforth, by the mere force of their own practice, they must attain Gnosis <note>The Greek term "Gnosis" is used here to translate the Sanskrit *Jnana*, meaning direct spiritual knowledge or realization of the Truth.</note>.

->That from which speech and the mind turn back, having failed to reach it; through practice, that pure Gnosis arises of its own accord. [180]<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "yato vāco nivartante aprāpya manasā saha | sādhanādamalaṃ jñānaṃ svayaṃ sphurati tadbhavam"</note>

180. That Gnosis, from which speech and the mind retreat baffled, is only to be obtained through practice; for then this pure Gnosis bursts forth by itself.

->There is no <term>Raja Yoga</term> <note>The "Royal Path" of yoga, focusing on meditation and mastery of the mind.</note> without <term>Hatha Yoga</term> <note>The physical path of yoga, focusing on postures (asanas) and breath control (pranayama).</note>, nor is there <term>Hatha Yoga</term> without <term>Raja Yoga</term>. Therefore, the Yogi should practice <term>Hatha Yoga</term> following the path of a true <term>Guru</term> <note>A spiritual teacher or guide.</note>. [181]<-

181. The *Hatha Yoga* cannot be obtained without the *Raja Yoga*, nor can the *Raja Yoga* be attained without the *Hatha Yoga*. Therefore, let the Yogi first learn the *Hatha Yoga* from the instructions of the wise Guru.

->While the body is stable and alive, if one does not firmly apply themselves to Yoga, they are living merely for the sake of enjoying the objects of the senses—of this there is no doubt. [182]<-

182. He who, while living in this physical body, does not practice Yoga, is living merely for the sake of sensual enjoyments.

->Until the practice reaches full maturity, a moderate diet should be one's refuge. Otherwise, even a wise man cannot succeed in his spiritual discipline here. [183]<-

183. From the time he begins until the time he gains perfect mastery, let the Yogi eat moderately and abstemiously; otherwise, however clever, he cannot gain success.

->In an assembly, the wise one should speak words of the highest virtue. He should act to preserve his physical frame while avoiding excessive talk. Company must be renounced, renounced—utterly renounced at all times. Otherwise, <term>mukti</term> <note>Spiritual liberation or freedom from the cycle of rebirth.</note> cannot be attained; I tell you the truth, the absolute truth. [184]<-

184. The wise Yogi in an assembly should utter words of highest good, but should not talk much. He eats a little to keep up his physical frame; let him renounce the company of men, let him renounce the company of men, verily, let him renounce all company: otherwise he cannot attain *mukti* (salvation); verily, I tell you the truth.

->Practice should be performed in secrecy, having abandoned company. For the sake of worldly dealings, one may engage with others as if by external habit. All beings abide in their own actions, which are born of past deeds. If one acts merely as an instrument, no fault ever arises. [185]<-

185. <note>This verse explains that while the Yogi must internally renounce the world to practice, they may still perform their social duties externally without accruing "karma" (spiritual debt), provided they remain detached and act only as an instrument of a higher power.</note>

<summary>This page emphasizes that higher spiritual knowledge (Gnosis) arises only through diligent practice and the balance of physical (Hatha) and mental (Raja) Yoga. It provides practical lifestyle advice for the Yogi, including moderation in food, speech, and the necessity of internal solitude.</summary>

<keywords>Gnosis, Jnana, Hatha Yoga, Raja Yoga, Guru, mukti, diet, renunciation, moderation, karma</keywords>

### CHAPTER V. 185-191.

185. Let him practice this in secrecy, away from the company of others, in a secluded place. For the sake of social appearances, he should remain within society, but he should not let his heart be attached to it. He should not abandon the duties of his profession, social class <note>original: "varna" or caste</note>, or stage of life <note>original: "ashrama" or rank</note>; rather, let him perform these simply as an instrument of the Lord, without any concern for the outcome. By acting in this way, no sin is incurred.

->Having decided this with a wise mind, even a house-holder who acts accordingly,<-
->attains success; on this point, no further deliberation is needed. 186.<-

186. Even the house-holder (<term>grihastha</term>—one who lives a family life), by wisely following this method, may obtain success <note>attainment of spiritual perfection</note>; there is no doubt about it.

->Released from both merit and demerit, having cast off all attachments,<-
->he who remains in the house is liberated, while remaining a house-holder.<-
->The house-holder united in Yoga is never stained by merit or demerit,<-
->even if he performs "sins" in the course of his duties for the welfare of the world. 187.<-

<margin>1</margin>
187. Remaining in the midst of the family, always performing the duties of the house-holder, he who is free from the concepts of merits and demerits <note>the karmic results of good and bad deeds</note>, and has restrained his senses, attains salvation. The house-holder practicing Yoga is not touched by sins; even if he must perform an action that seems sinful for the protection of mankind <note>original: "loka-sangraha" — the maintenance of the world</note>, he is not polluted by it.

->*The Sacred Mantra* **Om Aim Klim Hrim** <note>These are "seed syllables" or Bija Mantras representing different divine energies.</note><-

->Now I shall describe the most excellent practice of the Mantra,<-
->by which happiness in this world and the next is quickly attained. 188.<-

188. Now I shall tell you the best of practices, the repetitive chanting (<term>japa</term>) of a <term>mantra</term> (sacred sound): from this, one gains happiness in this life as well as in the world beyond this.

->When this excellent mantra is known, success in Yoga is certainly achieved;<-
->it grants all lordly power and pleasure to the great practitioner of Yoga. 189.<-

189. By knowing this highest of the <term>mantras</term>, the Yogi certainly attains success (<term>siddhi</term>): this gives all power and pleasure to the one-pointed Yogi.

->In the Root-Support center there is a lotus possessed of four petals;<-
->in its center is the "Seed of Speech," flashing with the brilliance of lightning. 190.<-

190. In the four-petalled <term>Muladhara</term> <note>the energy center at the base of the spine</note> lotus is the <term>bija</term> (seed-syllable) of speech, brilliant as lightning (this refers to the syllable **Aim** <term>aim</term>).

->In the heart is the "Seed of Desire," glowing like the Bandhuka flower;<-
->In the eyebrow-lotus is the "Seed of Power," shining like a million moons.<-
->These three seeds are secret, granting the fruits of both worldly enjoyment and liberation.<-
->The Yogi seeking success should practice these three mantras. 191.<-

<note>This final verse explains the locations for the other parts of the mantra: "Klim" is placed in the heart center, and "Hrim" is placed in the Ajna or third-eye center.</note>

<summary>This page explains that house-holders can achieve spiritual liberation through detached action and introduces a powerful three-part mantra (Aim, Klim, Hrim) associated with the body's energy centers.</summary>
<keywords>House-holder (Grihastha), Yoga, Mantra, Muladhara, Siddhi, Liberation, Bija (Seed-syllable), Aim, Klim, Hrim</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for mantra practice and ritual worship of the Goddess within the Hatha Yoga tradition. It follows previous descriptions of physical postures and energy centers.</meta>

# SHIVA SAMHITA

191. In the heart resides the <term>bîja</term> <note>a "seed-syllable" or fundamental sound used in meditation</note> of love, as beautiful as the *bandhuka* flower <note>original Sanskrit: *klīṃ*</note>. In the space between the eyebrows—specifically in the <term>Âjña</term> <note>the "command" chakra or energy center located at the third eye</note> lotus—is the <term>bîja</term> of <term>Śakti</term> <note>Divine Feminine Power; original Sanskrit: *strīṃ*</note>, as brilliant as tens of millions of moons. These three seeds should be kept secret—they provide both worldly enjoyment and spiritual liberation. Let the <term>Yogi</term> <note>yoga practitioner</note> repeat these three <term>mantras</term> <note>sacred chants</note> and strive to attain success.

<note>Translator's Note: The mystical names of these seed-syllables are not explicitly named in the verse itself. The complete sequence referred to is *Om, aim, klīm, strīm*.</note>

->Having received this mantra from the teacher, let him chant neither too fast nor too slowly.<-
->Connecting letter to letter, he should chant with a mind free from doubt. // 192 //<-

192. Let him learn this <term>mantra</term> from his <term>Guru</term> <note>spiritual teacher</note>; let him repeat it neither too fast nor too slowly, keeping the mind free from all doubts, and understanding the mystical relationship between the letters of the <term>mantra</term>.

->Absorbed in that and single-minded, the wise one follows the method of the scriptures.<-
->Before the Goddess, having performed one hundred thousand fire offerings, he should chant it three hundred thousand times. // 193 //<-

193. The wise <term>Yogi</term>, intently fixing his attention on this <term>mantra</term> and performing the duties specific to his path, should perform one hundred thousand <term>homs</term> <note>ritual fire sacrifices</note> and then repeat this <term>mantra</term> three hundred thousand times in the presence of the Goddess <term>Tripura</term> <note>meaning "of the three cities," a name for the supreme Divine Mother</note>.

->With oleander flowers, sugar, milk, and clarified butter mixed together,<-
->The wise one should perform the fire sacrifice in a triangular altar at the end of the chanting. // 194 //<-

194. At the end of this sacred repetition (<term>japa</term>), let the wise <term>Yogi</term> again perform a <term>hom</term> <note>fire offering</note> in a triangular-shaped <note>original: *yoni*-shaped, representing the source of creation</note> hollow, using sugar, milk, butter, and the flower of the *karavira* <note>oleander</note>.

->When the ritual is performed, the wise one has completed the preliminary service.<-
->Then the Goddess Tripura Bhairavi truly grants all desires. // 195 //<-

195. By this performance of sacrifice and chanting, the Goddess <term>Tripura Bhairavi</term>, who has been propitiated by the above <term>mantra</term>, becomes pleased and grants all the desires of the <term>Yogi</term>.

->Having pleased the Guru and properly received the best of mantras,<-
->One who follows this method attains success, even if they have little merit. // 196 //<-

196. Having satisfied the <term>Guru</term> and having received this highest of <term>mantras</term> in the proper way, and performing its repetition in the manner laid down with a concentrated mind, even one heavily burdened with past <term>Karmas</term> <note>the accumulated weight of past actions</note> or poor fortune attains success.

<keywords>bîja, mantra, Âjña chakra, Śakti, Yogi, Guru, fire sacrifice, Tripura Bhairavi, japa, karma, oleander</keywords>

### CHAPTER V. 197-203. 85

->The practitioner who has conquered their senses should chant the mantra one hundred thousand times. At the sight of him, women become agitated with desire; they fall before the practitioner, shameless and free from fear. 197.<-

197. The <term>Yogi</term> <note>a practitioner of yoga</note>, who having controlled his senses, repeats this <term>mantra</term> <note>a sacred sound or syllable used in meditation</note> one hundred thousand times, gains the power of attracting others.

->By chanting it two hundred thousand times, those beings established in their own spheres come to him like pilgrims to a holy site, abandoning their family ties. They surrender all they possess to him and remain under his control. 198.<-

198. By repeating it two *lacs* <note>a lac is 100,000</note> of times he can control all persons—they come to him as freely, as women go to a pilgrimage. They give him all that they possess, and remain always under his control.

->By chanting it three hundred thousand times, the rulers of the various regions along with their provinces come under his power; there is no doubt of this. 199.<-

199. By repeating this *mantra* three *lacs* of times, all the deities presiding over the spheres as well as the spheres, are brought under his dominion.

->By chanting it six hundred thousand times, he gains control over the king of the earth, along with his servants, armies, and vehicles. 200.<-

200. By repeating this six *lacs* of times, he becomes the vehicle of power—yea, the protector of the world—surrounded by servants.

->By chanting twelve hundred thousand times, the lords of the Yakshas, Rakshasas, and Nagas come under his control; they all constantly obey his commands. 201.<-

201. By repeating this twelve *lacs* of times, the lords of <term>Yakshas</term> <note>nature spirits associated with wealth and trees</note>, <term>Râkshas</term> <note>powerful supernatural beings, often guardians or demons</note> and the <term>Nâgas</term> <note>semi-divine serpent beings</note> come under his control; all obey his command constantly.

->By chanting fifteen hundred thousand times, the wise master practitioner brings the Siddhas, Vidyadharas, Gandharvas, and Apsaras under his control. There is no doubt about this. Through this force, he immediately attains the power of hearing distant sounds and omniscience. 202.<-

202. By repeating this fifteen *lacs* of times, the <term>Siddhas</term> <note>perfected masters with supernatural powers</note>, the <term>Viddyâdharâs</term> <note>magical celestial beings</note>, the <term>Gandharvas</term> <note>celestial musicians</note>, and the <term>Apsarâs</term> <note>celestial nymphs or dancers</note> come under the control of the <term>Yogi</term>. There is no doubt of it. He attains immediately the knowledge of all audition <note>the ability to hear sounds from any distance or dimension</note> and thus all-knowinghood.

->By chanting eighteen hundred thousand times, the practitioner can rise from the earth with this very body; he attains a divine, luminous body. He travels the world at his own will and can see through the very earth itself. 203.<-

203. By repeating this eighteen *lacs* of times, he, in this body, can rise from the ground: he attains verily the luminous body; he goes all over <note>the text breaks off here and continues on the following page</note>

<note>original: "mantra, lac, Yogi, Siddha, Viddhyâdharâ, Gandharva, Apsarâ, Yaksha, Râksha, Nâga, all-knowinghood, luminous body"</note>

<summary>This page outlines the specific results of chanting a mantra between 100,000 and 1,800,000 times, ranging from personal attraction to the subjugation of celestial beings and the attainment of a luminous body capable of flight.</summary>
<keywords>mantra yoga, japa, siddhi, supernatural powers, lac, Yakshas, Siddhas, luminous body, levitation, omniscience</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the supernatural powers (siddhis) attained through mantra repetition and the necessity of keeping these practices secret. It concludes a section on the specific rewards of massive counts of recitation.</meta>

# SHIVA SAMHITA

...the universe, wherever he likes; he sees the pores of the earth, *i.e.*, he sees the interspaces and the molecules of this solid earth.

->By repeating this <term>twenty-eight hundred thousand<note>original: "अष्टाविंशतिभिर्लक्षैर्" (28 lacs); a lac is 100,000</note></term> times, he becomes the lord of the <term>wisdom-holders<note>original: "Viddyâdharâs"; a class of celestial beings in Hindu mythology</note></term>;<-
->the wise practitioner becomes <term>one who can take any form<note>original: "kâma-rûpi"</note></term> and possesses great strength.<-
->By repeating this <term>thirty hundred thousand<note>original: "त्रिशल्लक्षै" (30 lacs)</note></term> times, he becomes equal to the <term>Creator and the Preserver<note>original: "Brahmâ" and "Vishnu"</note></term>.<-
->He becomes the <term>Destroyer<note>original: "Rudratvam"; the state of Rudra or Shiva</note></term> by sixty hundred thousand repetitions, and attains immortality by eighty hundred thousand.<-
->By <term>ten million<note>original: "कोट्यैकया" (one crore)</note></term> repetitions, the great yogi is absorbed into the <term>Supreme State<note>original: "parame pade"</note></term>.<-
->Such a practitioner becomes a yogi who is exceedingly rare to find in the <term>three worlds<note>The physical, ancestral, and heavenly realms</note></term>. 204.<-

204. By repeating this 28 *lacs* of times, he becomes the lord of the *Viddyâdharâs*, the wise Yogi becomes *kâma-rûpi* (*i.e.*, can assume whatever form he desires.) By repeating these thirty *lacs* of times he becomes equal to *Brahmâ* and *Vishnu*. He becomes a Rudra, by sixty *lac* repetitions, by eighty *lac* repetitions he becomes all-enjoyer, by repeating one tens of millions of times, the great Yogi is absorbed in the <term>Supreme Reality<note>original: "Param Brahman"</note></term>. Such a practitioner is hardly to be found throughout the three worlds.

->In the three worlds, there is but one Shiva, the destroyer of the <term>three cities<note>original: "Tripura"; refers to a famous myth where Shiva destroys three demonic fortresses</note></term>, the highest cause.<-
->That state is unchanging, peaceful, immeasurable, and free from all illness.<-
->Without a doubt, the wise man attains everything he desires. 205.<-

205. O Goddess! Shiva, the destroyer of Tripura, is the One first and the Highest cause. The wise attains Him, who is unchanging, undecaying, all peace, immeasurable and free from all ills—the Highest Goal.

->The science of Shiva is a <term>great science<note>original: "mâhâvidyâ"</note></term>, and it was kept secret in the past, O Great Goddess.<-
->This scripture spoken by me must therefore be kept secret by the wise. 206.<-

206. O great Goddess! this science of Shiva is a great science (*mâhâvidyâ*), it had always been kept secret. Therefore, this science revealed by me, the wise should keep secret.

->The <term>science of forceful yoga<note>original: "Haṭha-vidya"</note></term> should be kept strictly secret by the practitioner desiring success.<-
->It becomes powerful when kept secret and loses its power when revealed. 207.<-

207. The Yogî, desirous of success, should keep the <term>Haṭha Yoga<note>A system of physical techniques designed to channel vital energy</note></term> as a great secret. It becomes fruitful while kept secret, revealed it loses its power.

->The wise person who reads this daily from beginning to end,<-
->undoubtedly attains success in Yoga by degrees.<-
->The wise one who honors this daily attains <term>liberation<note>original: "moksha"; release from the cycle of rebirth</note></term>. 208.<-

208. The wise one, who reads it daily from beginning to end, undoubtedly, gradually obtains success in Yoga. He attains emancipation who honors it daily.

<summary>This page details the specific spiritual and supernatural rewards for repeating sacred mantras millions of times, eventually leading to union with the Supreme Reality. It also emphasizes that Yoga is an esoteric "science" that must be kept secret to maintain its efficacy and potency.</summary>

<keywords>mantra repetition, siddhis, shape-shifting, Shiva, Hatha Yoga secrecy, liberation, Brahma, Vishnu, Rudra, Mahavidya</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page concludes Chapter V of the Shiva Samhita, continuing the discussion on the accessibility of Yoga for those living domestic lives.</meta>

### CHAPTER V. 209--212.

---

->This knowledge should be recited to all holy men who seek <term>liberation</term><note>original: "moksha", the ultimate freedom from the cycle of rebirth</note>. Success comes through consistent practice; how could it ever be achieved without effort? // 209.<-

209. Let this science be recited to all holy men, who desire emancipation. By practice success is obtained, without it how can success follow.

->Therefore, the best of Yogis should practice Yoga according to the prescribed rules of the tradition. A householder who is content with what they receive by chance, who has mastered their inner senses, and who remains unattached to worldly duties, surely attains <term>liberation</term> through the practice of Yoga. // 210.<-

210. Therefore, the Yogis should perform Yoga according to the rules of practice. He who is contented with what he gets, who restrains his senses, being a house-holder, who is not absorbed in the house-hold duties, certainly attains emancipation by the practice of Yoga.

->Even wealthy or powerful householders can achieve success through <term>meditative recitation</term><note>original: "japa", the rhythmic repetition of a sacred mantra or name</note>, provided they apply themselves to the practices of Yoga. Therefore, a householder should strive in their Yoga practice (for their wealth and social status are not obstacles to spiritual progress). // 211.<-

211. Even the lordly house-holders obtain success by *japa*, if they perform the duties of Yoga properly. Let, therefore, a house-holder also exert in Yoga (his wealth and condition of life are no obstacles in this.)

->Though living in a home surrounded by children and a spouse, if one abandons attachment and walks the inner path of Yoga, that householder will see the signs of success. By following my teachings and letting go of the sense of "mine," they will live in true happiness. // 212.<-

212. Living in the house amidst wife and children, but being free from attachments to them, practising Yoga in secrecy, a house-holder even finds marks of success (slowly crowning his efforts), and thus following this teaching of mine, he ever lives in blissful happiness.

->Thus ends the fifth chapter of the glorious *Shiva Samhita*, a scripture on Yoga presented as a <term>dialogue between Shiva and Parvati</term><note>original: "Haragauri Samvada"; Shiva is often called Hara and Parvati is called Gauri</note>. // 5. May there be auspiciousness.<-

<summary>This concluding section of Chapter V emphasizes that Yoga is not only for ascetics but is fully attainable for householders who practice with detachment and consistency. It highlights that social status and family life are not barriers to spiritual liberation.</summary>
<keywords>liberation, householder, japa, Shiva Samhita, detachment, success in yoga, Shiva and Parvati dialogue</keywords>
